-convergence of
\{(A_{k_{n}},{S_{{k_{n}}}})\}
, the fact that
{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{n}}:=}\overline{S_{h_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}}}=S_{h_{k_{n}}}
, and the properties of Kuratowski convergence, it follows that
{\widetilde{S}_{n}:=}S_{h_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}S_{h}
. On the other hand, let
x\in\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h,K})
, since

{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}})={\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}}})\setminus\widetilde{K}_{n}\subset{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}}})\setminus K_{k_{n}}={\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})

and by the fact that
\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h,K})
, there exists

x_{n}\in\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\subset\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}})

such that
x_{n}\to x
. Now, we consider a sequence
x_{n}\in\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}})
converging to a point
x\in\Omega
. We proceed by contradiction, namely we assume that
x\in{\rm{Int}}(S_{h,K})
. Therefore, there exists
\epsilon>0
such that
{\rm{sdist}}(x,\partial S_{h,K})=-\epsilon
, which implies that
{\rm{sdist}}(x,\partial S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\to-\epsilon
as
n\to\infty
. Thus, there exists
n_{\epsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
, such that
x_{n}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(x)\subset{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})
, for every
n\geq n_{\epsilon}
. However, notice that

(4.15)

\begin{split}x_{n}\in\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}\left(S_{h_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}}\right)&=\Omega\setminus\left({\rm{Int}}({S_{h_{k_{n}}}})\setminus\widetilde{K}_{n}\right)\\
&=\left(\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}\left(S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}}\right)\right)\cup\left(\left(\partial\widetilde{A}_{n}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}}\right)\cap{\overline{{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}}})}}\right),\end{split}

where in the last equality we used the definition of
\widetilde{K}_{n}:=K_{k_{n}}\cup((\partial\widetilde{A}_{n}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\cap{\overline{{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}}})}})
and the fact that
{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})={\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k_{n}}})\setminus K_{k_{n}}
. Therefore, by (
4.15
) we deduce that
x_{n}\in\partial\widetilde{A}_{n}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}}
for every
n\geq n_{\epsilon}
and hence,
x\in\partial A
by (a2) and Remark
3.8
-(i). We reached an absurd as it follows that
x\in{\rm{Int}}(S_{h,K})\cap\partial A=\emptyset
. This conclude the proof of (
4.14
) and hence, of the claim.

By (
3.15
) and by conditions (a1), (a4) and (
4.13
), we observe that

(4.16)

\lim_{n\to\infty}{\absolutevalue{\mathcal{S}(A_\kn, S_\kn) - \mathcal{S}(\widetilde{A}_n,\wt S_n) }}=\lim_{n\to\infty}{\absolutevalue{\mathcal{S}(A_\kn, S_{h_\kn,K_\kn}) - \mathcal{S}(\widetilde{A}_n,S_{h_\kn,\widetilde{K}_n}) }}=0,

and

(4.17)

\mathcal{W}(A_{k_{n}},u_{k_{n}})=\mathcal{W}(\widetilde{A}_{n},u_{k_{n}}).

By (
3.17
), (
4.11
), (
4.13
), (
4.17
), (a3) and thanks to the fact that
\mathcal{S}
is non-negative, we obtain that

\int_{E_{i}^{n}}{\absolutevalue{e(u_\kn)}^{2}dx}\leq\int_{\widetilde{A}_{n}}{\absolutevalue{e(u_\kn)}^{2}dx}\leq\frac{R}{2c_{3}},

for every
i\in I
and for
n
large enough. Therefore, by a diagonal argument and by
[
36
, Corollary 3.8]
(applied to, with the notation of
[
36
]
,
P=E_{i}
and
P_{n}=E_{i}^{n}
) up to extracting not relabelled subsequences both for
\{u_{{k_{n}}}\}\subset H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\{E_{i}^{n}\}_{n}
there exist
w_{i}\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{{loc}}}(E_{i},\mathbb{R}^{2})
, and a sequence of rigid displacements
\{b_{n}^{i}\}
such that
(u_{k_{n}}+b_{n}^{i})\mathbbm{1}_{E^{n}_{i}}\rightarrow w_{i}
a.e. in
E_{i}
. Let
\{D^{n}_{i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{I}}
for an index set
\widetilde{I}
be the family of open and connected components of
\widetilde{A}_{n}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in I}{E_{i}^{n}}
such that by (a3)
{\rm{Int}}(D^{n}_{i})
converges to the empty set for every
i\in\widetilde{I}
. In
D_{i}^{n}
we consider the null rigid displacement, and we define

{b}_{n}:=\sum_{i\in I}{b_{n}^{i}}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{i}^{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad u:=\sum_{i\in I}{w_{i}\mathbbm{1}_{E_{i}}}.

We have that
u\in H^{1}_{\mathrm{{loc}}}(\mathrm{{Int}}(A);\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
{b}_{n}
is a rigid displacement associated to
\widetilde{A}_{n}
,
u_{k_{n}}+{b}_{n}\rightarrow u
a.e. in
\mathrm{{Int}}(A)
and hence,
{(A,S,u)=}(A,S_{h,K},u)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
and
{(\widetilde{A}_{n},\widetilde{S}_{n},u_{k_{n}}+{b}_{n}):=}(\widetilde{A}_{n},S_{{h}_{k_{n}},\widetilde{K}_{n}},u_{k_{n}}+{b}_{n})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\mathcal{C}}}(A,S_{h,K},u)
.
Furthermore, as
e(u_{k_{n}}+b_{n})=e(u_{k_{n}})
, from (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) it follows that

\begin{split}&{\lim_{n\to\infty}{\absolutevalue{\mathcal{F}(A_\kn,S_{h_{k_n},K_{k_n}}
,u_\kn) - \mathcal{F}(\widetilde{A}_n,\wt S_n, u_\kn+ b_n) }}}\\
&=\lim_{n\to\infty}{\absolutevalue{\mathcal{F}(A_\kn,S_{h_\kn,K_\kn},u_\kn) - \mathcal{F}(\widetilde{A}_n,S_{h_\kn,\widetilde{K}_n}, u_\kn+ b_n) }}=0,\end{split}

which implies (
3.21
) and completes
the proof.
∎

5.
Lower semicontinuity

In this section we prove that for any fixed
\mathbf{m}:=(m_{0},m_{1})\in\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
the energy
\mathcal{F}
is lower semicontinuous in the family of configurations
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
with respect to the topology
\tau_{\mathcal{C}}
. Since
\mathcal{F}
is given as the sum of the surface energy
\mathcal{S}
and the elastic energy
\mathcal{W}
, we proceed by proving that both
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{W}
are independently lower semicontinuous with respect to
\tau_{\mathcal{C}}
.

We begin with
\mathcal{S}
and we adopt
Fonseca-Müller blow-up technique

[
29
]
, for which we make use of a localized version
{\mathcal{S}}_{L}
of the surface energy, which we can consider with surface tensions constant with respect to the variable in
\overline{\Omega}
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\phi_{\text{F}},\phi_{\text{S}},\phi_{\text{FS}}
be three functions and let
\phi:=\min\{\phi_{\mathrm{S}},\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}\},\,\phi^{\prime}:=\min\{\phi_{\mathrm{F}},\phi_{\mathrm{S}}\}
be such that
\phi_{\text{F}},\,\phi_{\text{FS}},\,\phi\,,\phi^{\prime}\in C(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,\infty])
are Finsler norms and the hypotheses (H1) and (H2) are satisfied by the functions
\varphi_{\alpha}{,\varphi}{,\varphi^{\prime}}\in C(\overline{\Omega}\times\mathbb{R}^{2})
given for
\alpha=\text{S},\text{FS},\text{F}
by
\varphi_{\alpha}(x,\cdot):=\phi_{\alpha}(\cdot)
,
\varphi(x,\cdot):=\phi(\cdot)
and
{\varphi^{\prime}(x,\cdot)=\phi^{\prime}(\cdot)}
for every
x\in\overline{\Omega}
.
We define the localized surface energy
{\mathcal{S}}_{L}:{\mathcal{B}_{L}}\to[0,+\infty]
by

(5.1)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A,S,O\right)&:=\int_{O\cap(\partial^{*}A\setminus\partial{S})}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{O\cap\partial^{*}{S}\cap\partial^{*}A}{\phi(\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{O\cap(\partial^{*}{S}\setminus\partial A)\cap A^{(1)}}{\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}(\nu_{S})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{O\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)}}{(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{O\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)}\cap S^{(0)}}{2\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}{+\int_{O\cap\partial A\cap A^{(0)}}{2\phi^{\prime}(\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}\\
&\quad+\int_{O\cap(\partial{S}\setminus\partial A)\cap\left(S^{(1)}\cup S^{(0)}\right)\cap A^{(1)}}{2\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}(\nu_{S})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{O\cap\partial{S}\cap\partial A\cap S^{(1)}}{2{\phi}(\nu_{S})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\end{split}

for every
(A,S,O)\in\mathcal{B}_{L}:=\{(A,S,O)\,:\,(A,S)\in\mathcal{B},\text{ $O$ open and contained in $\Omega$}\}
.

We start with some technical results needed in the blow-up argument used in Theorem
5.13
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
Q
be any open square,

K\subset\overline{Q}
be a nonempty closed set and
E_{k}\subset\overline{Q}
be such that
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial E_{k})\to{\rm dist}(\cdot,K)
uniformly in
\overline{Q}
as
k\to\infty
. Then
E_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}K
as
k\to\infty
. Analogously, if
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial E_{k})\to-{\rm dist}(\cdot,K)
uniformly in
\overline{Q}
as
k\to\infty
, then
\overline{Q}\setminus E_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}K
as
k\to\infty
.

The proof of the previous lemma follows from the same arguments of
[
36
, Lemma 4.2]
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and let
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a set such that
\partial E
has at most
m
-connected components, is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-rectifiable, and satisfies
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial E)<\infty
. Let
x\in\partial E
be such that the measure-theoretic unit normal
\nu_{E}\left(x\right)
of
\partial E
at
x
and there exists
R>0
such that
\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}\left(x\right)}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(E)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}\left(x\right)}\cap T_{x}
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
, where
T_{x}:=T_{x,\nu_{E}(x)}
. Then, the following assertions hold true:

(a)

If
x\in E^{(1)}\cap\partial E
, then
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(E))\to-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{x})
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}}
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
;

(b)

If
x\in E^{(0)}\cap\partial E
, then
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(E))\to{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{x})
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}}
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
;

(c)

If
x\in\partial^{*}E
then
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(E))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{x})
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}}
as
\rho\to 0^{+}
.

Proof.

The cases (a) and (b) follow directly from
[
36
, Proposition A.5]
. It remains to prove the case (c) to which the remaining of the proof is devoted. Let
x\in\partial^{*}E
and
R>0
be such that

(5.2)

\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}\left(x\right)}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(E)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{R,\nu_{E}\left(x\right)}\left(x\right)}\cap T_{x}\text{ as }\rho\rightarrow 0^{+}.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
x=0,\nu_{E}(0)=\mathbf{e}_{\mathbf{2}}
,
H_{x}=H_{0}
and
T_{x}=T_{0}=\partial H_{0}
.

Let
\{\rho_{k}\}\subset(0,1)
be such that
\rho_{k}\to 0
and let
f_{k}:={\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(\partial E))\big|_{Q_{R}}\in W^{1,\infty}(Q_{R})
. We see that for any
k>0
,
f_{k}
is
1
-Lipschitz continuous, moreover by the fact that
f_{k}(0)={\rm{sdist}}(0,\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(\partial E))=0
, we deduce that
\{f_{k}\}
is uniformly bounded. By applying Ascoli-Arzelà Theorem, there exists
f\in W^{1,\infty}(Q_{R})
and a non-relabeled subsequence
\left\{f_{k}\right\}
such that
f_{k}\to f
uniformly in
Q_{R}
. In view of
[
36
, Proposition A.1]
, by (
5.2
) we obtain that
\absolutevalue{f_k}={\rm dist}(\cdot,\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(\partial E))\to{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})
uniformly in
Q_{R}
and thus,
\absolutevalue{f(x)}={\rm dist}(x,\partial H_{0})
for any
x\in Q_{R}
.

It remains to prove that
f(\cdot)={\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})
in
Q_{R}
. We proceed by absurd. Assume by contradiction that
f\neq{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})
then, either
f\equiv{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial(Q_{R}\setminus H_{0}))
or
f\equiv{\rm dist}(x,T_{0})
or
f\equiv-{\rm dist}(x,T_{0})
. Let us first consider the case in which
f\equiv{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial(Q_{R}\setminus H_{0}))
. In view of Remark
3.8
-(i), it follows that
\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}Q_{R}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(H_{0})
and so, as a consequence we have that
\mathcal{L}^{2}\left(\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E)\triangle(Q_{R}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(H_{0}))\right)\to 0
as
k\to\infty
, which is in contradiction with the De Giorgi’s structure theorem of sets of finite perimeter (see
[
24
, Theorem 5.13]
or
[
43
, Theorem 15.5]
).

In the case
f(\cdot)\equiv{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})
, thanks to the fact that
f_{k}\to f
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{R}}
and by Lemma
5.2
, we have that
\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E)\cap\overline{Q_{R}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{R}}
, and thus,
\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E)}\to\mathbbm{1}_{T_{0}}
in
L^{1}(\overline{Q_{R}})
, which is a contradiction with the fact that
\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E)}\to\mathbbm{1}_{H_{0}}
in
L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
by
[
24
, Theorem 5.13]
.

In the last case in which
f(\cdot)\equiv-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})
, we proceed analogously, and by Lemma
5.2
we obtain that
\overline{Q_{R}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}
and hence,
\mathbbm{1}_{(\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E))}\to\mathbbm{1}_{T_{0}}
in
L^{1}(\overline{Q_{R}})
. Therefore, we reach a contradiction again by applying
[
24
, Theorem 5.13]
since
x\in\partial^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus E)
and so,
\mathbbm{1}_{(\overline{Q_{R}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(E))}\to\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(H_{0})}}
in
L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
.
∎

We now introduce the notions of
film free boundary
,
substrate free boundary
, and
film-substrate adhesion interface
for triples
(A,h,K)\in\mathcal{B}
, and of
triple junctions
at the points where they “meet”.

Definition 5.4
.

For any admissible pair
(A,S)\in{\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}}
we denote:

•

the
film free boundary
, the
substrate free
boundary and the
film-substrate adhesion interface
by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{F}}(A,S)

\displaystyle:=\left((\partial A\setminus\partial S)\cup(\partial S\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)}){\cup(\partial{S}\cap\partial^{*}A\cap S^{(0)})}\right)\cap{\Omega},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathrm{S}}(A,S)

\displaystyle:=\left((\partial S\cap\partial A)\setminus{(\partial{S}\cap\partial^{*}A\cap S^{(0)})}\right)\cap{\Omega},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S)

\displaystyle:=\left(\left((\partial S\setminus\partial A)\cap A^{(1)}\right)\right)\cap{\Omega},

respectively. Notice that the
film-substrate delamination interface
, that we define by

\Gamma^{\text{D}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S):=\left((\partial S\cap\partial A)\cap A^{(1)}\right)\cap{\Omega},

is contained both in
\Gamma_{\mathrm{F}}(A,S)
and in
\Gamma_{\mathrm{S}}(A,S)
.

•

triple junction
(by including for simplicity also the “double” junctions at the boundary) any point

\displaystyle p\in

\displaystyle\left(Cl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{F}}(A,S))\cap Cl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{S}}(A,S))\cap Cl(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S))\cap{\Omega}\right)

\displaystyle\cup\left(Cl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{F}}(A,S))\cap Cl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{S}}(A,S))\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S})\cap\partial{\Omega})\right)

\displaystyle\cup\left(Cl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{F}}(A,S))\cap Cl(\Gamma_{\mathrm{FS}}^{A}(A,S))\cap\partial{\Omega})\right),

where the closures are considered with respect to the relative topology of
\partial A\cup\partial S
.

The next result allows us to assume that the adhesion interface of any admissible pair
(A,S)\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
(without the substrate internal cracks) can be considered, up to an error and up to passing to the family
\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
, to be given by a finite number (depending on the initial pair
(A,S)
) of connected components.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
R
be an open rectangle with two sides, that are denoted by
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, perpendicular to
\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
. Let
(A,S_{h,K})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
for
(h,K)\in\mathrm{AHK}
be such that
{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h,K},R)<\infty
, where
{\mathcal{S}}_{L}
is the localized surface energy defined in
(
5.1
). If
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\left(\Gamma^{\mathrm{{A}}}_{\mathrm{FS}}\left(A,S_{h,K}\right)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h})\right)\cap R\right)>0
, for every
\eta\in(0,1)
small enough there exist
M:=M(A,S_{h,K},\eta)\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
such
that
(\Gamma^{\mathrm{{A}}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{S}}))\cap R
has at most
M
connected components and

(5.3)

{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h,K},R)\geq{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S},R)-\eta.

Furthermore,
(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})
satisfies the following properties:

(i)

If
\overline{\partial S_{h}\cap R}\cap T_{\ell}\neq\emptyset
for
\ell=1,2
, then also

\overline{\partial\widetilde{S}\cap R}\cap T_{\ell}\neq\emptyset
for
\ell=1,2
;

(ii)

If there exists a closed connected set
\Lambda\subset\overline{\partial A\cap R}
such that
\Lambda\cap T_{\ell}\neq\emptyset
for
\ell=1,2
, then there exists a curve with support
\widetilde{\Lambda}\subset\overline{\partial\widetilde{A}\cap R}
such that
\widetilde{\Lambda}\cap T_{\ell}\neq\emptyset
for
\ell=1,2
.

Proof.

Notice that we cannot a priori exclude that
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S_{h,K})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h}))\cap R
is a totally disconnected set with positive
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure (see, for instance, the
Smith-Volterra-Cantor set
in
[
47
, Chapter 3]
). We denote by
L(h)
the set of substrate filaments of the substrate
S_{h,K}
, namely,

(5.4)

L(h):=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\overline{\Omega}:x_{1}\in(-l,l)\text{ and }h^{+}(x_{1})<x_{2}\leq h(x_{1})\}\subset\partial S_{h}.

Since
h
is upper semicontinuous, there exist an index set
J_{1}
and a countable family of disjoint points
\{x_{1}^{j}\}_{j\in J_{1}}\subset(-l,l)
such that

(5.5)

L(h)=\bigcup_{j\in J_{1}}L_{j}(h),

where
L_{j}(h):=\{(x^{j}_{1},x_{2})\in\overline{\Omega}:h^{+}(x^{j}_{1})<x_{2}\leq h(x^{j}_{1})\}
for
every
j\in J_{1}
. In the following three
steps, in view of the outer regularity of Borel measures, we construct an admissible
pair
(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
by modifying some portions of
\partial S_{h}
and
\partial A
. More
precisely, in the first step, we construct an
admissible height
h^{1}
by eliminating a family of “small” filaments of
L(h)
so that
L(h^{1})
consists of only a finite number of filaments, we
accordingly modify
A
in an admissible region
A^{1}
containing
L(h)\setminus L(h^{1})
, and we define
K^{1}:=K
. In the second step, we construct
S^{2}
by modifying
S_{h^{1},K^{1}}

and we introduce an admissible region
A^{2}
in such a way that
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}}))\cap R
is a countable union of connected components, and (i) and (ii) hold true.
In the third step, by eliminating some components of
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}}))\cap R
we define an admissible pair
(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}

for which
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{S}}))\cap R
has at most
M
-connected components, (i) and (ii) are preserved, and (
5.3
) holds true.

Step 1 (Modification of substrate filaments).
We modify
(A,S_{h,K})
in
(A^{0},S_{h^{1},K^{1}})
to have a finite number of substrate filaments. We denote by
J_{2}\subset J_{1}
the set of indexes
j\in J_{1}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(L_{j}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(A))=0
and
L_{j}
is not connected to
\partial A
, and we denote by
F_{J_{2}}(h)
the set of the
x_{1}
-coordinates corresponding to the points in each vertical segment
L_{j}
for
j\in J_{2}
, i.e.,
F_{J_{2}}(h):=\{x^{j}_{1}\}_{j\in J_{2}}
, where
x^{j}_{1}\in{[-l,l]}
is such that
(x^{j}_{1},h(x^{j}_{1}))\in{L_{j}(h)}
and
h^{+}(x^{j}_{1})<h(x^{j}_{1})
for every
j\in J_{2}
.
We define as
{h^{0}}
the modification of
h
, given by

\begin{matrix}h^{0}:&{[-l,l]}&\to&{[0,L]}&\\
&x&\mapsto&h^{0}(x)&:=\begin{cases}h(x)&\text{if }x\in{[-l,l]}\setminus F_{J_{2}}(h),\\
h^{+}(x)&\text{if }x\in F_{J_{2}}(h),\end{cases}\end{matrix}

and observe that by construction
h^{0}\in\text{AH}
and

(5.6)

\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial S_{h^{0}})\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial S_{h}),

where
S_{h^{0}}
is defined as in (
3.2
). We notice that the triple
(A,S_{h^{0},K^{0}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
, where
K^{0}:=K
.
As a consequence of the construction and of the non-negativity of
\phi_{\text{FS}}
, it follows that

(5.7)

{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h,K},R)\geq{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h^{0},K^{0}},R).

We notice that by (
5.5
) we have that

\mathcal{H}^{1}(L(h^{0}))=\mathcal{H}^{1}(L(h))-\sum_{j\in J_{2}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(L_{j}(h))=\sum_{j\in J_{3}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(L_{j}(h)),

where
J_{3}:=J_{1}\setminus J_{2}
and
L(h^{0})
is the set of substrate filaments of
S_{h^{0},K^{0}}
defined as in (
5.4
). Therefore, for a fix
\widetilde{\eta}>0
there is
j^{\prime}_{1}:=j^{\prime}_{1}(\widetilde{\eta})\in J_{3}
such that

(5.8)

\sum_{j=j^{\prime}_{1}+1,j\in J_{3}}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}(L_{j}(h))\leq\widetilde{\eta},

and we define

A^{1}:=\left(A\setminus\left(\bigcup_{j=j^{\prime}_{1}+1,j\in J_{3}}^{\infty}(L_{j}(h)\cap{\rm{Int}}(A))\right)\right)\cup\bigcup_{j=j^{\prime}_{1}+1,j\in J_{3}}^{\infty}(L_{j}(h)\cap(\Omega\setminus\overline{{\rm{Int}}(A)})).

Furthermore, we denote by
h^{1}
the modification of
h^{0}
, defined by

\begin{matrix}h^{1}:&{[-l,l]}&\to&{[0,L]}&\\
&x&\mapsto&h^{1}(x)&:=\begin{cases}h^{0}(x)&\text{if }x\in{[-l,l]}\setminus F_{J_{4}}(h^{0}),\\
{h^{0}}^{+}(x)&\text{if }x\in F_{J_{4}}(h^{0}),\end{cases}\end{matrix}

where
J_{4}:=\{j\in J_{3}:j\geq j^{\prime}_{1}+1\}
and
F_{J_{4}}(h^{0}):=\{x^{j}_{1}\}_{j\in J_{4}}
such that

(x^{j}_{1},h(x^{j}_{1}))\in{L_{j}(h)}

for every
j\in J_{4}
.
We define
K^{1}:=K^{0}
, we notice that
(h^{1},K^{1})\in\text{AHK}
and
S_{h^{1},K^{1}}\subset\overline{A^{1}}
, thus
(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
and since
L_{j}(h)
is connected to
\partial A
for every
j\in J_{4}\subset J_{3}
,
we deduce that
\partial A^{1}
has at most
m_{1}
-connected components.
Finally, we observe that

(5.9)

{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h,K},R)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}},R)\geq-\sum_{j\in J_{4}}2\int_{L_{j}(h)}{\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{L_{j}(h)})d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\geq-2c_{2}\widetilde{\eta},

where we used the non-negativeness of
\phi
,
\phi_{\text{F}S}
and (
3.15
), and we observe that
(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
has a finite number of substrate filaments, more precisely, we denote by
j^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
the cardinality of the index set
J:=J_{3}\setminus J_{4}=\{j\in J_{3}:j\leq j^{\prime}_{1}\}
and we have that
L(h^{1})
is the union of
j^{\prime}
filaments, i.e.,

(5.10)

L(h^{1})=\bigcup_{j\in J}L_{j}(h),

where
L(h^{1})
is defined as in (
5.4
) with respect to the substrate
S_{h^{1},K^{1}}\in\text{AS}(\Omega)
.

Step 2 (Modification of the substrate free boundary).
Without loss of generality in the following we assume that
\partial{\rm{Int}}(S_{h^{1}})\cap\overline{L(h^{1})}\subset R
.
Since
\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}\partial{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}}
is a finite Borel measure and

\Gamma^{\text{A}}:=\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h^{1}})\right)\cap R

is a Borel set, by the outer regularity of measures (see
[
43
, Theorem 2.10]
), there exists an open set
O=O(\widetilde{\eta})\subset R
such that
\Gamma^{\text{A}}\subset O\cap\partial{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}}
and

(5.11)

\begin{split}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\widehat{\Lambda}\right)&=\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((O\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}})\setminus\Gamma^{\text{A}}\right)\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((O\cap\partial{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}})\setminus\Gamma^{\text{A}}\right)<\frac{2^{-5/2}}{j^{\prime}+1}\widetilde{\eta},\end{split}

where
\widehat{\Lambda}:=(O\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}}))\setminus\Gamma^{\text{A}}
and
j^{\prime}
is defined in the Step 1 as the number of filaments of
S_{h^{1}}
. Moreover, by using the notation introduced in (
3.2
) and the fact that
h^{1}\in\text{AH}(\Omega)
we conclude that

(5.12)

\partial{\rm{Int}}\left(\overline{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}}\right)=\partial{\rm{Int}}(S_{h^{1}})=\partial S_{{h^{1}}^{+}},

and hence, since
\partial\mathrm{{\rm{Int}}}(\overline{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}})
is a connected and compact set in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with finite
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure, by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
there exists a parametrization
r:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
of
\partial\mathrm{{\rm{Int}}}(\overline{S_{h^{1},K^{1}}})
whose support
\gamma
joins the points
(-l,{h^{1}}(-l^{+}))
with
(l,{h^{1}}(l^{-}))
.

Notice that by Step 1,
\partial{\rm{Int}}(S_{h^{1}})\cap\overline{L(h^{1})}
is the union of
j^{\prime}
-points that we can order by labeling them with
p_{1},\dots,p_{j^{\prime}}
. Furthermore, we denote with
p_{0}:=r(t_{0})
and
p_{j^{\prime}+1}:=r(t_{1})
, where
t_{0}:=\inf\{t\in[0,1]:r(t)\in\partial R\}
and
t_{1}:=\sup\{t\in[0,1]:r(t)\in\partial R\}
, and we consider the family
\{R^{i}\}_{i=1}^{j^{\prime}+1}
of the strips
R^{i}
defined as the open regions of
R
contained between the vertical lines passing though the points
p_{i-1}
and
p_{i}
.

Since
\widehat{\Lambda}
is a Borel measurable set, by (
2.6
) and (
5.11
) we have that

(5.13)

\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widehat{\Lambda})\leq 2^{\frac{5}{2}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widehat{\Lambda})<\frac{\widetilde{\eta}}{j^{\prime}+1},

where
\mathcal{N}^{1}
is the net measure defined in (
2.4
).
Therefore, there exists
\delta>0
such that we can find a family of disjoint dyadic squares
\{U_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{Q}
such that
\widehat{\Lambda}\subset\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}U_{n}
,
\mathrm{diam}(U_{n})\leq\delta
for any
{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and

(5.14)

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathrm{diam}(U_{n})}\leq\mathcal{N}^{1}(\widehat{\Lambda})+\frac{\widetilde{\eta}}{j^{\prime}+1}<\frac{2}{j^{\prime}+1}\widetilde{\eta}.

Without loss of generality we assume that (
\widehat{\Lambda}\neq\emptyset
and that)
U_{n}\cap\widehat{\Lambda}
is non-empty for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\{U_{n}^{i}\}\subset\{U_{n}\}
be the subfamily of dyadic squares such that
U_{n}^{i}\cap R^{i}\cap\gamma\neq\emptyset
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Furthermore, we assume for simplicity that
{\rm{Int}}(U_{n}^{i})\subset R^{i}
.
We begin by modifying the pair
(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}})
in the strip
R^{1}
, by denoting the modification by
(A^{2}_{1},S^{2}_{1})
. We characterize
A^{2}_{1}
and
S^{2}_{1}
as

(5.15)

A^{2}_{1}:=\left(A^{1}\cup\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{U_{n}^{1}}\right)\setminus\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\partial U_{n}^{1}\setminus{U_{n}^{1}})

and

(5.16)

S^{2}_{1}:=\left(S_{h^{1},K^{1}}\setminus\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\rm{Int}}(U_{n}^{1})\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\partial U^{1}_{n}\cap(R^{1}\setminus S_{h^{1}})),

respectively.
By construction, it follows that
S^{2}_{1}\subset\overline{A^{2}_{1}}
,
\partial A^{2}_{1}
and
\partial S^{1}_{1}
have finite
\mathcal{H}^{1}
measure and are
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-rectifiable, and
\partial A^{2}_{1}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}_{1}})=\emptyset
and hence,

(A^{2}_{1},S^{2}_{1})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
. Furthermore, we have that

(5.17)

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}},R^{1}\right)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{1}^{2},S_{1}^{2},R^{1}\right)\\
&\geq-2\int_{\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\partial U^{1}_{n}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})+\phi(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})+\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq-\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}2\int_{\partial U^{1}_{n}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})+\phi(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})+\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq-\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}24\,c_{2}\text{diam}(U^{1}_{n})\geq-\frac{48}{j^{\prime}+1}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used the non-negativeness of
\phi_{\mathrm{F}},\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}
and
\phi
, in the second inequality we used the subadditivity of measures, in the third inequality we used (
3.15
), and in the last inequality we used (
5.14
).
We notice that
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2}_{1},S^{2}_{1})\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}_{1}})\cap R^{1}
is a countable union of connected sets because by construction every connected component of
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2}_{1},S^{2}_{1})\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}_{1}})\cap R^{1}
is connected to an element in the family of sets
\{\partial U^{1}_{n}\cap U^{1}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Now, we modify
(A_{1}^{2},S_{1}^{2})
in a new configuration
(A^{3}_{1},S^{3}_{1})
in order to prove Assertion (ii). To this end let
\Lambda\subset\overline{\partial A\cap R}
be a closed connected set such that
\Lambda\cap T_{\ell}\neq\emptyset
for
\ell=1,2
.
By
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
there exists a parametrization
r_{1}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
whose support
\gamma^{1}\subset\Lambda
joins
T_{1}
with
T_{2}
. We define
\gamma^{1}_{1}:=\gamma^{1}\cap\overline{R^{1}}
and we observe that
(\gamma^{1}_{1}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(U^{1}_{n}))\cup\partial U^{1}_{n}
is a connected set. Let
Z_{1}\subset\mathbb{N}
be the set of indexes
n
such that
\gamma_{1}\cap(\partial U^{1}_{n}\cap U^{1}_{n})\neq\emptyset
.
If
Z_{1}=\emptyset
, then we define
A^{3}_{1}:=A^{2}_{1}

and
S^{3}_{1}:=S^{2}_{1}
.
If
Z_{1}\neq\emptyset
, then we modify
\gamma_{1}^{1}
in
\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}U^{1}_{n}
by defining a new set
\Lambda_{1}
. More precisely,
by using the fact that dyadic squares by definition do not intersect each other, we fix
n\in Z_{1}
and we replace with a set
\Lambda^{1}_{n}
(see (
5.24
) below) the portion of
\gamma^{1}
passing through
U^{1}_{n}
.
To this end, let us denote the closures of the left and bottom sides of
U^{1}_{n}
by
L^{1}_{n}
and
L^{2}_{n}
, respectively, and proceed by defining
\Lambda^{1}_{n}
in different way with respect to following three cases (see Figure
2
):

Case 1:

\bm{\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{1}_{n}\neq\emptyset}
and
\bm{\gamma_{1}\cap L^{2}_{n}=\emptyset}
.
Since
L^{1}_{n}
is closed, we deduce that
\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{1}_{n}
is closed. Therefore, there exist
a^{2}_{n}:=\max\{a\in\mathbb{R};(l_{n}^{1},a)\in\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{1}_{n}\}
and
b^{2}_{n}:=\min\{b\in\mathbb{R};(l_{n}^{1},b)\in\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{1}_{n}\}
, where
l^{1}_{n}
is the element in the singleton
\pi_{1}(L^{1}_{n})
. Since
\gamma_{1}
is parametrized by
r_{1}
, there exist
t_{n}^{1},t_{n}^{2}\in[0,1]
such that
p_{n,1}^{1}:=(l^{1}_{n},a^{2}_{n})=r_{1}(t_{n}^{1})
and
p_{n,1}^{2}:=(l^{1}_{n},b^{2}_{n})=r_{1}(t_{n}^{2})
, and by the continuity of
r_{1}
there exists
q_{n,1}^{1}\in\gamma^{1}_{1}\setminus U^{1}_{n}
such that
{\rm dist}(p_{n,1}^{1},q_{n,1}^{1})\leq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(U^{1}_{n})}{2}
.
If
\pi_{1}(q_{n,1}^{1})=l^{1}_{n}
, then we define
\widetilde{q}_{n,1}^{1}:=(l_{n}^{1}-\varepsilon,\pi_{2}(q_{n,1}^{1})-\varepsilon)
for a
\varepsilon>0
small enough such that
{\rm dist}(q_{n,1}^{1},\widetilde{q}_{n,1}^{1})\leq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(U^{1}_{n})}{2}
, otherwise we let
\widetilde{q}_{n,1}^{1}:=q_{n,1}^{1}
.
We denote by
\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{1}
the segment connecting
q_{n,1}^{1}
with
\widetilde{q}_{n,1}^{1}
, we denote by
\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{2}
the segment connecting
\widetilde{q}_{n,1}^{1}
with
p_{n,1}^{2}
, and we denote by
\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{3}
the segment connecting
p_{n,1}^{1}
with the vertex of
U^{1}_{n}
in
\partial U^{1}_{n}\setminus U^{1}_{n}
. Let
\Lambda^{1}_{n}:=\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{3}}
and observe that by construction it follows that

(5.18)

\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Lambda^{1}_{n})=\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{3}})\leq{2\,}\text{diam}(U^{1}_{n}).

Case 2:

\bm{\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{1}_{n}=\emptyset}
and
\bm{\gamma_{1}\cap L^{2}_{n}\neq\emptyset}
.
By arguing analogously to Case 1 there exist

t_{n}^{1},t_{n}^{2}\in[0,1]
such that
p_{n,2}^{1}:=(a^{1}_{n},l^{2}_{n})=r_{1}(t_{n}^{1})
and
p_{n,2}^{2}:=(b^{1}_{n},l^{2}_{n})=r_{1}(t_{n}^{2})
, where
\pi_{2}(L^{2}_{n})=\{l^{2}_{n}\}
,
a^{1}_{n}:=\max\{a\in\mathbb{R};(a,l_{n}^{2})\in\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{2}_{n}\}
, and
b^{1}_{n}:=\min\{b\in\mathbb{R};(b,l_{n}^{2})\in\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{2}_{n}\}
. By the continuity of
r_{1}
there exists
q_{n,2}^{1}\in\gamma^{1}_{1}\setminus U^{1}_{n}
such that
{\rm dist}(p_{n,2}^{1},q_{n,2}^{1})\leq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(U^{1}_{n})}{2}
.
If
\pi_{2}(q_{n,2}^{1})=l^{2}_{n}
, then we define
\widetilde{q}_{n,2}^{1}:=(\pi_{1}(q_{n,2}^{1})-\varepsilon,l_{n}^{2}-\varepsilon)
for
\varepsilon>0
small enough such that
{\rm dist}(q_{n,2}^{1},\widetilde{q}_{n,2}^{1})\leq\frac{\mathrm{diam}(U^{1}_{n})}{2}
, otherwise we let
\widetilde{q}_{n,2}^{1}:=q_{n,2}^{1}
. We denote by
\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{1}
the segment connecting
\widetilde{q}_{n,2}^{1}
with
p_{n,2}^{1}
,
we denote by
\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{2}
the segment connecting
\widetilde{q}_{n,2}^{1}
with
p_{n,2}^{2}
and we denote by
\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{3}
the segment connecting
p_{n,2}^{1}
with with the vertex of
U^{1}_{n}
in
\partial U^{1}_{n}\setminus U^{1}_{n}
. Let
\Lambda^{1}_{n}:=\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{3}}
and observe that by construction it follows that

(5.19)

\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Lambda^{1}_{n})=\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{3}})\leq{2\,}\text{diam}(U^{1}_{n}).

Case 3:

\bm{\gamma^{1}_{1}\cap L^{1}_{n}\neq\emptyset}
and
\bm{\gamma_{1}\cap L^{2}_{n}\neq\emptyset}
.

We define
p^{k}_{n,\ell},q^{1}_{n,\ell},\widetilde{q}_{n,\ell}^{1}
,
\widetilde{L}_{n,\ell}^{\alpha}
for
k=1,2
,
\alpha=1,2,3
as in Case 1 for
\ell=1
and as in Case 2 for
\ell=2
. Furthermore, we denote by
{\widetilde{L}_{n}^{4}}
the segment connecting
p^{2}_{n,1}
with
p^{2}_{n,2}
. Let
\Lambda^{1}_{n}:=\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{2}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}^{3}_{n,1}\cup\widetilde{L}^{3}_{n,2}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n}^{4}}
and observe that by construction it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Lambda^{1}_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,1}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}^{3}_{n,1}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{1}\cup\widetilde{L}_{n,2}^{2}{\cup\widetilde{L}^{3}_{n,2}})+{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{L}^{4}_{n})}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\leq{5}\,\text{diam}(U^{1}_{n}).

Figure 2
.
The three squares appearing in the illustration represent dyadic squares
U^{1}_{n}
of the first strip
R^{1}
in the three different cases, namely, by moving from the left to the right, Cases 1, 2, and 3, that are considered in Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
5.5
. On the left the initial pair
(A^{2}_{1},S^{2}_{1})
is represented, while on the right the pair
(A^{3}_{1},S^{3}_{1})
that is obtained after the modification described in such step is depicted.

Let

\Gamma_{1}:=\overline{\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(\overline{A^{2}_{1}},S^{2}_{1})\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}_{1}})\cap R^{1}\right)\setminus\overline{\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}(U^{1}_{n})}}.

We now observe that the previous construction of
\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}\Lambda^{1}_{n}
does not divide
\Gamma_{1}
in an uncountable number of connected components. More precisely, we claim that for every given a connected component
\widehat{\Gamma}
of
\Gamma_{1}
,
\widehat{\Gamma}\setminus\overline{\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}\Lambda^{1}_{n}}
is a countable union of disjoint connected sets. To prove this claim, we notice that, since
\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}\subset\gamma
and
\gamma
is parameterized by
r
, also
\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}
is parametrizable and hence, there exists a continuous injective map
\widehat{r}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
whose support coincides with
\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}
. This in particular proves that
\widehat{r}
is a homeomorphism between
[0,1]
and
\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}
. The claim then follows from the fact that
\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}\setminus\overline{\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}\Lambda^{1}_{n}}
is open with respect to the relative topology of
\overline{\widehat{\Gamma}}
and
[
47
, Proposition 8 in Part 1]
.

We are now in the position to define
A^{3}_{1}
and
S^{3}_{1}
as follows

(5.21)

A^{3}_{1}:=\overline{A^{2}_{1}}\setminus\left(\left(\gamma^{1}\setminus\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}U_{n}^{1}\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(\partial U_{n}^{1}\setminus{U_{n}^{1}})\cup\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}(\overline{A^{2}_{1}}\cap\Lambda^{1}_{n})\right)\\
\cup\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}((R^{1}\setminus{\rm{Int}}({A^{2}_{1}}))\cap\Lambda^{1}_{n})

and

(5.22)

S^{3}_{1}:=S^{2}_{1}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}(\overline{A^{2}_{1}}\cap\Lambda^{1}_{n})\right).

Therefore, we have that

(5.23)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{1}^{2},S_{1}^{2},R^{1}\right)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{1}^{3},S_{1}^{3},R^{1}\right)&\geq-2\int_{\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}\Lambda^{1}_{n}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{A_{3}^{2}})+\phi(\nu_{A_{3}^{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq-\sum_{n\in Z_{1}}2\int_{\Lambda^{1}_{n}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})+\phi(\nu_{U^{1}_{n}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq-\sum_{n\in Z_{1}}{20}\,c_{2}\text{diam}(U^{1}_{n})\geq-\frac{{40}}{j^{\prime}+1}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used the non-negativeness of
\phi_{\mathrm{F}},\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}
and
\phi
, in the second inequality we used the subadditivity of measures, in the third inequality we used (
3.15
) and (
5.18
)–(
Case 3:
), and in the last inequality we used (
5.14
).

Moreover, by construction we have that

(5.24)

\Lambda_{1}:=\left(\gamma^{1}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}U_{n}^{1}\right)\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in Z_{1}}\Lambda^{1}_{n}\subset\partial A^{3}_{1}

is closed, connected, and joins
T_{1}
with
T_{2}
.

We now modify the pair
{(A^{3}_{1},S^{3}_{1})}
and
\Lambda_{1}
in the strip
R^{2}
, first by employing the same construction of (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) to obtain a configuration
{(A^{2}_{2},S^{2}_{2})}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
, and then by employing the same construction of (
5.21
) and (
5.22
)
to modify the pair
{(A^{2}_{2},S^{2}_{2})}
, by denoting the final modified pair with
{(A^{3}_{2},S^{3}_{2})}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
. We then define

(5.25)

\Lambda_{2}:=\left(\Lambda_{1}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{n\in Z_{2}}U_{n}^{2}\right)\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in Z_{2}}\Lambda^{2}_{n}\subset\partial A^{3}_{2}.

By iterating the same procedure on the strips
R^{i}
for
i=3,\ldots,j^{\prime}+1
we obtain the pair
(A^{2},S^{2}):=(A^{3}_{j^{\prime}+1},S^{3}_{j^{\prime}+1})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
and we define
\Lambda_{j^{\prime}+1}
as in (
5.25
) by replacing all the index 1 and 2 with
j^{\prime}
and
j^{\prime}+1
, respectively.
We observe that by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
there exists a support
\widetilde{\Lambda}\subset\Lambda_{j^{\prime}+1}
of a curve joining
T_{1}
with
T_{2}
.

Furthermore, as done in (
5.23
) for i=1,

(5.26)

{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{i}^{2},S_{i}^{2},R^{i}\right)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{i}^{3},S_{i}^{3},R^{i}\right)\geq-\frac{{40}}{j^{\prime}+1}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta}

for every
i=1,\ldots,j^{\prime}+1
.
Therefore, by iteration it follows that

(5.27)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}},R\right)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A^{2},S^{2},R\right)&\geq\sum_{i=1}^{j^{\prime}+1}\left({\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A^{1},S_{h^{1},K^{1}},R^{i}\right){-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{i}^{2},S_{i}^{2},R^{i}\right)}\right.\\
&\qquad\left.{+{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{i}^{2},S_{i}^{2},R^{i}\right)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}\left(A_{i}^{3},S_{i}^{3},R^{i}\right)}\right)\\
&\geq-\sum_{i=1}^{j^{\prime}+1}\frac{{88}}{j^{\prime}+1}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta}\\
&\geq-{88}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta},\end{split}

where in the second inequality we used (
5.17
) and (
5.26
).
We notice that
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}})\cap R
is a countable union of connected sets because by construction every connected component of
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}})\cap R
is connected to an element in the family of sets
\{\partial U_{n}\cap U_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. Therefore,
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}}))\cap R
is equal to a countable union of connected sets. More precisely, there exists
a family of connected and disjoint sets
\{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
such that

(5.28)

(\Gamma_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}}))\cap R=\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i},

and hence,

(5.29)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((\Gamma_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}})\cap R\right)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}\right).

We conclude this step by observing that Assertion (i) follows by the construction of
S^{2}
, while Assertion (ii) holds with the defined set
\widetilde{\Lambda}
.

Step 3 (From countable to a finite number of components).

Since
\mathcal{H}^{1}((\Gamma_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}}))\cap R)\leq\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial S^{2})<\infty
, by (
5.29
)
there exists
i_{0}:=i_{0}(\widetilde{\eta})\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.30)

\sum_{i=i_{0}+1}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i})\leq\widetilde{\eta}.

Notice that

(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}
, where
\widetilde{S}:=S^{2}
and
\widetilde{A}:=A^{2}\setminus\bigcup_{i\in\widetilde{I}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}
with
\widetilde{I}:=\{i\in\mathbb{N}:i\geq i_{0}+1\}
. Furthermore, it follows from Steps 1 and 2 that

(5.31)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h,K},R)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S},R)&={\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A,S_{h,K},R)\pm{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A^{2},S^{2},R)-{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S},R)\\
&\geq-{88}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta}-2\sum_{i=i_{0}+1}^{\infty}\int_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}})+\phi(\nu_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{i}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq-{92}c_{2}\widetilde{\eta},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used (
5.9
) and (
5.27
), and the definition of
\widetilde{A}
and the non-negativeness of
\phi_{\text{FS}}
and,
in the second inequality we used (
3.15
) and (
5.30
).
We conclude this step by defining
M\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
as the cardinality of
\mathbb{N}\setminus\widetilde{I}
, and we notice by construction that

(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(\widetilde{A},\widetilde{S})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{S}}))\cap R
has at most
M
-connected components.

Finally, we observe that Assertion (i) is a direct consequence of the construction in Steps 1 and 2, while Assertion (ii) follows from the definition of
\widetilde{\Lambda}
in Step 2 (which is not modified in Step 3 since
\widetilde{\Lambda}\cap(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A^{2},S^{2})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S^{2}}))=\emptyset
). The proof of
this lemma is concluded
by taking
\widetilde{\eta}:=\frac{\eta}{{92}c_{2}}
in (
5.31
) with
\eta\in(0,\min\{1,{92}c_{2}\})
.
∎

We formalize below the notions of
film islands
,
composite voids
, and
substrate grains
for any admissible pair
(A,S_{h,K})\in\mathcal{B}
.

Definition 5.6
.

Let
R\subset\Omega
be an open rectangle and let
(A,S)\in\mathcal{B}
. We refer to:

•

any closed component
V{\subset\overline{R}}
of
\Omega\setminus{\rm{Int}}(A)
such that

\partial V\cap(\Gamma_{\mathrm{S}}(A,S)\cup\Gamma^{\text{D}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S))
is not empty and it consists in one and only one connected component as an
extended
(as we also include “connected delamination regions”)
composite void
of the configuration
(A,S)
(or sometime for simplicity of the film region or of
A
).

•

any open connected component
P{\subset R}
of
{\rm Int}(A\setminus\overline{S})
such that
\partial P\cap\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S)
is not empty and it consists in one and only one connected component as a
film island
of the configuration
(A,S)
(or sometime for simplicity of the film region or of
A
), and to a film island
P={\rm Int}(A\setminus\overline{S})\cap R
of
(A,S)
as the
full island
of
A
.

•

any open connected component
G{\subset R}
of
{\rm Int}(S)
such that
\partial G\cap\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(A,S)
is not empty and it consists in one and only one connected component as a
substrate grain
of the configuration
(A,S)
(or sometime for simplicity of the substrate region or of
S
), and to a substrate grain
G={\rm Int}(S)\cap R
of
(A,S)
as the
full grain
of
S
.

The following results can be seen as analogous of
[
36
, Lemmas 4.4 and 4.5]
though in our more involved setting of three free interfaces (see Table
1
), where we have to distinguish among the blow-ups at:

-

the substrate free boundary (Lemma
5.7
),

-

the film-substrate incoherent (delaminated) interface (Lemma
5.8
),

-

the substrate cracks in the film-substrate incoherent interface (Lemma
5.9
),

-

the filaments of both the substrate and the film (Lemma
5.10
),

-

the substrate filaments on the film free boundary (Lemma
5.11
),

-

the delaminated substrate filaments in the film (Lemma
5.12
).

The strategy employed in these proofs is based on reducing to the situation of a finite number of connected components for the film-substrate coherent interfaces by Lemma
5.5
and then on designing induction arguments (with respect to the index of such components) in which we “shrink” islands, “fill” voids, and modify “grains” in new voids (see Figures
3
,
4
, and
6
, respectively) by means of the
minimality of segments
(see
[
43
, Remark 20.3]
).

We begin by addressing the setting of the substrate free boundary.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
R\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be an open rectangle with a side parallel to
\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
and let
T\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a line such that
T\cap R\neq\emptyset
. Let
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1]
be such that
\rho_{k}\searrow 0
and
R\subset\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega)
. If
\{(A_{k},{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}})\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega))
is a sequence such that

\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap{\overline{R}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}{T\cap\overline{R}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
(\overline{A_{k}}\setminus{\rm{Int}}\left(S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\right))\cap{\overline{R}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}{T\cap\overline{R}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
small enough, there exists
k_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.32)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},{{R}})\geq\int_{T\cap{\overline{R}}}{\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\delta

for any
k\geq k_{\delta}
.

Proof.

We prove (
5.32
) in three steps. In the first step, we prove (
5.32
) for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}:=(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k},K_{k}})){\cap R}
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible by following the program of
[
36
, Lemma 4.4]
. In the second step, we consider those
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}
has
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-positive measure and observe that in view of Lemma
5.5
we can pass, up to a small error in the energy, to a triple
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
such that
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}:=(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})){\cap R}
has
M_{k}
connected components, and which then is shown to always admit either
an island or a void. Finally, in the third step, we apply the anisotropic minimality of segments to prove (
5.32
) by means of an induction argument based on shrinking the islands and/or filling the voids of the triple
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})
.

If
T
is a vertical segment we define
c_{\theta}:=1
, otherwise we define
c_{\theta}:=(1/\sin\theta)+(1/\cos\theta)
, where
\theta<\pi/2
is the smallest angle formed by the direction of
T
with
\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
. Since
\overline{A_{k}}\setminus{\rm{Int}}({S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}){\cap\overline{R}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}{T\cap\overline{R}}
, for every
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
there exits
k_{\delta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.33)

\emptyset\neq\overline{A_{k}}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k},K_{k}}){\cap\overline{R}}\subset{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}

for any
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
, where
T^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in R:\text{dist}(x,T)<\delta^{\prime}/(2c_{\theta})\}
is the tubular neighborhood of
T
in
R
.

Step 1.
Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\right)=0
for a fix
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Since

(5.34)

\partial{\rm{Int}}\left(\overline{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\right)=\partial{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k}})=\partial S_{h_{k}^{+}},

by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
there exists a parametrization
r_{k}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
of
{\partial\mathrm{{\rm{Int}}}\left(\overline{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\right)}
, whose support we denote by
\gamma_{k}
. Notice that by (
5.33
), there exists

p_{0}:=r_{k}(t_{0})
and
p_{1}:=r_{k}(t_{1})
, where
t_{0}:=\inf\{t\in[0,1]:r_{k}(t)\in\partial R\}
and
t_{1}:=\sup\{t\in[0,1]:r_{k}(t)\in\partial R\}
, and also by (
5.34
)
t_{0}<t_{1}
. Let
\widetilde{T}_{i}\subset\partial R\cap\overline{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}
for
i=0,1
be the closed and connected set with minimal length connecting
p_{i}
with
T\cap\overline{R}
. Therefore, by trigonometric identities we obtain that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{T}_{i})\leq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2c_{\theta}}c_{\theta}=\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
.
We define
\Lambda_{k}:=(\partial R\cap\overline{T^{\delta^{\prime}}})\cap(\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(\Omega)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k},K_{k}}))
and we observe that

(5.35)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},{R}){+\int_{\Lambda_{k}}\phi(\nu_{\Lambda_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad\geq\int_{\gamma_{k}{\cap R}\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}}{\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\gamma_{k}{\cap R}\cap\partial^{*}S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap\partial A_{k}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}}{\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\qquad\qquad{+\int_{\Lambda_{k}}\phi(\nu_{\Lambda_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad=\int_{\Gamma_{k}}{\phi(\nu_{\Gamma_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\sum_{i=0}^{1}{\int_{\widetilde{T}_{i}}{\phi({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}},\end{split}

where
\Gamma_{k}:=\widetilde{T}_{0}\cup{(\gamma_{k}\cap\overline{R})\cup\Lambda_{k}}\cup\widetilde{T}_{1}
. By the anisotropic minimality of segments (see
[
43
, Remark 20.3]
), it yields that

(5.36)

\int_{\Gamma_{k}}{\phi(\nu_{\Gamma_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\geq\int_{{T\cap\overline{R}}}{\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},

and so, thanks to the facts that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{T}_{0}\cup\widetilde{T}_{1})\leq\delta^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Lambda_{k})\leq\delta^{\prime}
,
and by (
3.15
), (
5.33
)-(
5.36
), we deduce that

(5.37)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)\geq\int_{{T\cap\overline{R}}}{\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{2}c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Step 2.
Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})>0
for a fixed
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
. By applying Lemma
5.5
, with
\Omega=\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(\Omega))
, there exist

M_{k}:=M_{k}(\delta^{\prime},A_{k},h_{k},K_{k})\in\mathbb{N}
and an admissible triple
({\widetilde{A}^{1}_{k}},{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma_{\rho_{k}}(\Omega))
such that
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}

has
M_{k}
connected components and

(5.38)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}_{L}({\widetilde{A}^{1}_{k}},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})-c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

We consider
\widetilde{A}_{k}:={\widetilde{A}^{1}_{k}}\cup((\partial\widetilde{A}_{k}^{1}\setminus\partial\widetilde{S}_{k})\cap(\widetilde{A}^{1}_{k})^{(1)})
. By (
5.38
) and by the non-negativeness of
\phi_{\mathrm{F}}
we deduce that

(5.39)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}_{L}({\widetilde{A}_{k}},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})-c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

We denote by
\{\Gamma^{j}\}_{j=1}^{M_{k}}
the family of open connected components of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
.
In this step we prove that there exists at least an island or an extended void (see definition in (
5.6
)) in
\widetilde{A}_{k}
. More precisely, by arguing by contradiction we prove that one of the following two cases always applies:
M_{k}\geq 1
and there exists at least an island in
\widetilde{A}_{k}
, or
M_{k}\geq 2
and there exists at least an extended void in
\widetilde{A}_{k}
.

To this end, assume that the admissible pair
(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}})
does not present any island or extended void. We begin by observing that, since
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k})>0
, there exist a
j_{0}\in\{1,\ldots,M_{k}\}
and an open connected component
F_{1}
of
{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\cap R
such that
\Gamma^{j_{0}}\subset\partial F_{1}
. By the contradiction hypothesis, since

F_{1}
cannot be an island of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
, there exists
j_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,M_{k}\}\setminus\{j_{0}\}
such that
\Gamma^{j_{1}}\subset\partial F_{1}
and
\Gamma^{j_{0}}\cap\Gamma^{j_{1}}=\emptyset
.
Furthermore, as by the contradiction hypothesis there cannot be also an extended void between
\Gamma^{j_{0}}
and
\Gamma^{j_{1}}
, and by using the fact that
\partial\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}
contains
\Gamma^{j_{0}}
and
\Gamma^{j_{1}}
, and consists of only
one component, we conclude that there exist an open connected component
F_{2}
of
{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\cap R
, which could coincide or not with
F_{1}
, and at least an extra
j_{2}\in\{1,\ldots,M_{k}\}\setminus\{j_{0},j_{1}\}
such that
\Gamma^{j_{2}}\subset\partial F_{2}
.

We now claim that there exist an open connected component
F_{3}
of
{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\cap R
, which could or not coincide with
F_{2}
, and at least an extra
j_{3}\in\{1,\ldots,M\}\setminus\{j_{0},j_{1},j_{2}\}
such that
\Gamma^{j_{3}}\subset\partial F_{3}
. Indeed, if
F_{1}\neq F_{2}
, the claim is a direct consequence of applying to
F_{2}
the same argument applied to
F_{1}
to find
\Gamma^{j_{1}}
, and we define
F_{3}=F_{2}
,
while, if
F_{2}=F_{1}
, the claim is a direct consequence of applying to
F_{2}
with the pair of components consisting, e.g., of
\Gamma^{j_{0}}
and
\Gamma^{j_{2}}
, the same argument applied to
F_{1}
with the pair of components
(\Gamma^{j_{0}},\Gamma^{j_{1}})
to find
\Gamma^{j_{2}}
, with
F_{3}
being possibly, but not necessary, equal to
F_{2}
.

Moreover, the same reasoning applied on
F_{2}
, can be implement also on
F_{3}
, yielding an open connected component
F_{4}
of
{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\cap R
and at least an extra
j_{4}\notin\{j_{n}\}_{n=0,\dots,3}
such that
\Gamma^{j_{4}}\subset\partial F_{4}
. As such, by keeping on iterating
this reasoning we reach a contradiction with the fact that the family of connected components of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
consists of at most
M_{k}<\infty
elements.

Step 3.
In this step we prove (
5.32
) for those
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})>0
, which together with Step 1 concludes the proof of (
5.32
). More precisely, we prove that

(5.40)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R)\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-6c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

which, in view of (
5.39
), yields Assertion (i) by taking
\delta^{\prime}:=\frac{\delta}{7c_{2}}
and
k_{\delta}:=k_{\delta^{\prime}}
for any
\delta\in(0,\min{\{7c_{2},1\}})
.

In order to prove (
5.40
)
we consider an auxiliary energy
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}
in
\mathcal{B}
by defining

(5.41)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R):=\mathcal{S}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R){+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{T^{k}_{i}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\partial R})+\phi(\nu_{\partial R})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

for every
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
, where
T^{k}_{1}
and
T^{k}_{2}
are the closed connected components of
\partial R\cap\overline{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}
,
we recall that
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
has
M_{k}
connected components, and we prove that

(5.42)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R)\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-2c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

by proceeding by induction on the number
M_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
of connected components of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
in three steps. Notice that (
5.40
) directly follows from (
5.42
), since

\mathcal{H}^{1}(T^{k}_{i})\leq\delta^{\prime}

by (
5.33
) and the definition of
T^{k}_{i}
and hence,

(5.43)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R)\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R)-4c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

by (
3.15
).

In Substeps 3.1 and 3.2 we prove the basis of the induction by proving it in both the two cases provided by Step 2, i.e., if
M_{k}=1
and
\widetilde{A}_{k}
presents an island, and if
M_{k}=2
and
\widetilde{A}_{k}
presents an extended void, respectively. Finally in Substep 3.3 we prove the induction and obtain (
5.42
).

Step 3.1
. We consider the basis of the induction in the case in which
M_{k}=1
and there is an island
P_{1}{\subset R}
of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
such that
\Gamma^{1}\subset\partial P_{1}
.
Let
p_{1},p_{2}\in\partial P_{1}
be two different
triple junctions
of
P_{1}
(see Definition
5.4
) such that
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
belong to the relative boundary of
\Gamma^{1}
in
\partial{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}
and let

L_{1}
by the closed segment connecting
p_{1}
with
p_{2}
.
It follows that

{\widehat{\Gamma}}:=(\partial{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}\setminus\Gamma^{1})\cup L_{1}

is connected and closed. We consider by
\{P^{1}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
the family of open connected components enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\Gamma^{1}
. We are now in the position to characterize the modification
\widehat{S}_{k}
of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
by

\widehat{S}_{k}:=\left(\widetilde{S}_{k}\setminus\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N},P^{1}_{n}\subset\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}\overline{P^{1}_{n}}\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N},P^{1}_{n}\subset R\setminus{{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{S}_{k})}}\overline{P^{1}_{n}}.

Furthermore, we consider by
\{P^{2}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
the family of open connected components enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\partial P_{1}\setminus\Gamma^{1}
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\partial P^{2}_{n}\cap\Gamma^{1}
and
\partial P^{2}_{n}\setminus\Gamma^{1}
have one non-empty connected component. We define

\widehat{A}_{k}:=\left(\widetilde{A}_{k}\setminus\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N},P^{2}_{n}\subset\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}}\overline{P^{2}_{n}}\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N},P^{2}_{n}\subset R\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{A}_{k})}\overline{P^{2}_{n}}

(see Figure
3
). By applying the anisotropic minimality of segments (see
[
43
, Remark 20.3]
), it yields that

(5.44)

\begin{split}&\int_{(\partial P_{1}\setminus\Gamma^{1})\cap\partial^{*}{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus{\partial\widetilde{S}_{k}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{(\partial P_{1}\setminus\Gamma^{1})\cap\left(\partial\widetilde{A}_{k}\setminus{\partial\widetilde{S}_{k}}\right)\cap\left({\widetilde{A}_{k}}^{(0)}\cup{\widetilde{A}_{k}}^{(1)}\right)}{2\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{\Gamma^{1}\cap\left(\partial^{*}{\widetilde{S}_{k}}\setminus\partial{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\right)\cap{\widetilde{A}_{k}}^{(1)}}{\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{{\widetilde{S}_{k}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\Gamma^{1}\cap\left({\partial\widetilde{S}_{k}}\setminus\partial{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\right)\cap{\widetilde{S}_{k}^{(0)}}\cap{\widetilde{A}_{k}}^{(1)}}{2\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{{\widetilde{S}_{k}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\partial P_{1}\setminus\Gamma^{1})\cap{\partial\widetilde{S}_{k}}\cap\partial^{*}\widetilde{A}_{k}\cap{\widetilde{S}_{k}^{(0)}}}{{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}}(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{(\partial P_{1}\setminus\Gamma^{1})\cap(T^{k}_{1}\cup T^{k}_{2})}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\partial P_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\geq\int_{L_{1}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{L_{1}})+\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}(\nu_{L_{1}})\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\geq\int_{{L_{1}}}{\phi(\nu_{L_{1}})\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used the definition of
\phi
.
We notice that the last term in the left side of the previous inequality is needed to include in the analysis the situation in which
\overline{P_{1}}\cap\partial{R}\neq\emptyset
.
From (
5.44
), the inequality (
5.42
) directly follows as

(5.45)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},R)\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{2}c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

where in the last inequality we used the non-negativeness of
\phi_{\mathrm{F}}
and we proceeded by applying Step 1 to the configuration
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k}\right)
for which by construction it holds that
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}\left(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k}\right)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}}))\cap R
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible.

Figure 3
.
The two illustrations above represent, passing from the left to the right, the construction that consists in “shrinking” a film island, which is contained in Step 3.1 of the proof of Lemma
5.7
for the basis of the induction in the case with
M=1
and with
\widetilde{A}_{k}
presenting an island
P_{1}
.

Step 3.2
. To conclude the basis of the induction, we consider the case with
M=2
and the presence of an extended void
V_{1}{\subset\overline{R}}
of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
. Let
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
be the two triple junctions such that
p_{1},p_{2}\in\partial V_{1}
and
p_{i}\in\Gamma^{i}
, for
i=1,2
. By
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
there exists a curve with support
\gamma^{1}\subset\partial V_{1}\cap\partial{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}
connecting
p_{1}
with
p_{2}
. Furthermore, by
[
36
, Lemma 4.3]
, since
\partial V_{1}
is connected,
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-finite and
V_{1}
is bounded, there exists a curve with
support
\gamma^{2}\subset\overline{\partial V_{1}\setminus(\gamma^{1}\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{V_{1}})}
. Notice that
\gamma^{1}
and
\gamma^{2}
can intersect only in the delamination area, or more precisely
\gamma^{1}\cap\partial^{*}{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}\cap\partial^{*}\widetilde{A}_{k}
and
\gamma^{2}\cap\partial^{*}\widetilde{A}_{k}\setminus\partial^{*}{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}
are disjoint up to a

\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible set. We denote by
L_{1}
the closed segment connecting
p_{1}
with
p_{2}
, notice that

{\widehat{\Gamma}}:=\overline{(\partial{\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}\setminus\gamma^{1})}\cup L_{1}.

is connected and closed. We consider by
\{V^{1}_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
the family of open connected components enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\partial V_{1}\cap\partial\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\partial V^{1}_{n}\cap\partial\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}
and
\partial V^{1}_{n}\cap L_{1}
have one non-empty connected component. We characterize the modification
\widehat{S}_{k}
of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
as

\widehat{S}_{k}:=\left(\widetilde{S}_{k}\setminus\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N},V^{1}_{n}\subset\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}{\overline{V^{1}_{n}}}\right)\cup\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N},V^{1}_{n}\subset R\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{S}_{k})}V^{1}_{n}.

Furthermore, we define

\widehat{A}_{k}:=\widehat{A}_{k}\cup({\rm{Int}}{(\widehat{S}_{k})}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}).

We notice that by construction

(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega_{k})
and
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}}\right)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}})=\Gamma_{1}\cup L_{1}\cup\Gamma_{2}
is a connected set (see Figure (
4
)). Moreover, by the anisotropic minimality of segments (see
[
43
, Remark 20.3]
), it follows that

(5.46)

\begin{split}&\int_{\gamma^{2}\cap\partial^{*}{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus{\partial{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\gamma^{1}\cap\partial^{*}{\widetilde{S}_{k}}\cap\partial^{*}{\widetilde{A}_{k}}}{\phi(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\gamma^{2}\cap\partial^{*}{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\cap{\widetilde{S}_{k}^{(0)}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\gamma^{1}\cup\gamma^{2})\cap\partial^{*}{{\widetilde{S}_{k}}}\cap\partial\widetilde{A}_{k}\cap{\widetilde{A}_{k}}^{(1)}}{(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\nu_{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\geq\int_{L_{1}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{L_{1}})+\phi(\nu_{L_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\geq\int_{L_{1}}{\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}(\nu_{L_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used (
3.16
). We now obtain (
5.42
) by observing that

(5.47)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{2}c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

where in the first inequality we used
(
5.46
) and in the second inequality we proceed by applying Step 3.1 to the configuration
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)
, which by construction presents a island and is such that
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}})){\cap R}
consists of one and only component.

Figure 4
.
The two illustrations above represent, passing from the left to the right, the construction that consists in “filling” a void, which is contained in Step 3.2 of the proof of the Lemma
5.7
for the basis of the induction in the case with
M=2
and with
\widetilde{A}_{k}
presenting a void
V_{1}
.

Step 3.3
.
Now we make the inductive hypothesis that (
5.42
) holds true if

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
has
M_{k}=j-1
connected components, and we prove that (
5.42
) holds also if
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
has
M_{k}=j
connected components. We observe that by Step 2 we have the two cases:

(a)

j\geq 1
and there exists at least an island
P{\subset R}
in
\widetilde{A}_{k}
;

(b)

j\geq 2
and there exists at least an extended void
V{\subset\overline{R}}
in
\widetilde{A}_{k}
.

In the case (a) we proceed by applying the same construction done in Step 3.1 with respect to the island
P
instead of
P_{1}
obtaining the configuration
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega_{k})
. We observe that by construction
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}})){\cap R}
presents
j-1
connected components (since a component is canceled) and hence, we obtain that

(5.48)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{2}c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

where we used (
5.45
) in the first inequality and we applied the induction hypothesis on
(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}})
in the second.

In the case (b) we proceed by applying the same construction done in Step 3.2 with respect to the extended void
V
instead of
V_{1}
obtaining the configuration
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
. We observe that by construction
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega_{k})
and
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\mathrm{FS}}\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}})){\cap R}
presents
j-1
connected components (since two components are connected in one). Finally, we have that

(5.49)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{2}c_{2}\delta^{\prime}

where we used (
5.47
) in the first inequality and we applied the induction hypothesis on
(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}})
in the second.
∎

We continue with the setting of the film-substrate incoherent delaminated interface.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
R\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be an open rectangle with a side parallel to
\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
and let
T\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be a line such that
T\cap R\neq\emptyset
and let
x\in T
. Let
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1]
be such that
\rho_{k}\searrow 0
and
R\subset\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega)
.
If
\{(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega))
is a sequence such that
{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\overline{R}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}{H_{x,\nu_{T}}}\cap\overline{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\overline{R}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T\cap\overline{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
small enough, there exists
k_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.50)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{T})+\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\delta.

for any
k\geq k_{\delta}
.
The same statement remains true if we replace
H_{x,\nu_{T}}
with
H_{x,-\nu_{T}}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{S}_{L}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)<\infty
.
We prove (
5.50
) in two steps. In the first step, we prove (
5.50
) for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}:=(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k},K_{k}})){\cap R}
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible by repeating the same arguments of Step 1 in the proof of Lemma
5.7
. In the second step, by arguing as in
[
36
, Lemma 4.4]
we prove (
5.50
) for those
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})
is positive.

If
T
is a vertical segment we define
c_{\theta}:=1
, otherwise we define
c_{\theta}:=(1/\sin\theta)+(1/\cos\theta)
, where
\theta<\pi/2
is the smallest angle formed by the direction of
T
with
\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
. Since
\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\overline{R}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T\cap\overline{R}
and
\overline{R}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T\cap\overline{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, for every
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
there exits
k_{\delta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.51)

\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap\overline{R},\,\overline{R}\setminus A_{k}\subset{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}

for every
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
, where
T^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in R:\text{dist}(x,T)<\delta^{\prime}/(2c_{\theta})\}
is a tubular neighborhood of
T
in
R
. By arguing as in (
5.34
)
there exists a parametrization
r_{k}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
of
{\partial\mathrm{{\rm{Int}}}\left(\overline{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\right)}
, whose support we denote by
\gamma_{k}
. Finally, let
T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{1}
and
T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{2}
be the connected components of
\partial R\cap\overline{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}
.

Step 1.
Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\right)=0
for a fix
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Notice that by (
5.51
), there exists

p_{1}:=r_{k}(t_{1})
and
p_{2}:=r_{k}(t_{2})
, where
t_{1}:=\inf\{t\in[0,1]:r_{k}(t)\in\partial R\}
and
t_{2}:=\sup\{t\in[0,1]:r_{k}(t)\in\partial R\}
, and without loss of generality we assume that
t_{1}<t_{2}
. Let
\widetilde{T}^{1}_{i}\subset\partial R\cap{T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}}
for
i=1,2
be the closed and connected set with minimal length connecting
p_{i}
with
T\cap\overline{R}
. Therefore, by trigonometric identities we obtain that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{T}_{i})\leq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2c_{\theta}}c_{\theta}=\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}
. Since
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\right)=0
and by
[
36
, Lemma 4.3]
, there exists a curve with
support
\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}\subset\overline{\partial A_{k}\setminus(\gamma^{1}\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{A_{k}})}
such that
\gamma_{k}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}
can intersect only in the delamination area, more precisely,
\gamma_{k}\cap\partial^{*}S_{h_{k}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\setminus\partial S_{h_{k}}
are disjoint up to a

\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible set. We define
\Lambda_{k}:=(\partial R\cap\overline{T^{\delta^{\prime}}})\cap(\Omega_{k}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k},K_{k}}))
and
we denote by
\widetilde{T}^{2}_{i}\subset\partial R\cap{T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}}
for
i=1,2
be the closed and connected set with minimal length connecting
\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}
with each point of
T\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}
. It yields that

(5.52)

\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},{R}){+\int_{\Lambda_{k}}\phi(\nu_{\Lambda_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad\geq\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k}}\setminus{\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\gamma_{k}\cap\partial^{*}S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k}}}{\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\qquad+\int_{(\gamma_{k}\cup\widetilde{\gamma}_{k})\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial A_{k}\cap{A_{k}}^{(1)}}{(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\Lambda_{k}}\phi(\nu_{\Lambda_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\quad=\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\Gamma_{k}}{\phi(\nu_{\Gamma_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}{\int_{\widetilde{T}^{1}_{i}}{\phi(\nu_{\widetilde{T}^{j}_{i}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}-\sum_{i=1}^{2}{\int_{\widetilde{T}^{2}_{i}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{T}^{j}_{i}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}},\end{split}

where
\Gamma_{k}:=\widetilde{T}^{1}_{1}\cup{(\gamma_{k}\cap\overline{R})\cup\Lambda_{k}}\cup\widetilde{T}^{1}_{2}
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}:=\widetilde{T}^{2}_{1}\cup\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}\cup\widetilde{T}^{2}_{2}
. By the anisotropic minimality of segments (see
[
43
, Remark 20.3]
), it yields that

(5.53)

\int_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{k}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\Gamma_{k}}{\phi(\nu_{\Gamma_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\geq\int_{{T\cap\overline{R}}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},

and so, thanks to the facts that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{T}^{j}_{0}\cup\widetilde{T}^{j}_{1})\leq\delta^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Lambda_{k})\leq\delta^{\prime}
for
j=1,2
,
and by (
3.15
), (
5.51
)-(
5.53
), we deduce that

(5.54)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)\geq\int_{{T\cap\overline{R}}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{3}c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Step 2.

Since
(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{k}(\Omega))
we can find an enumeration
\{\Lambda^{n}_{k}\}_{n=1,\ldots,m_{k}^{1}}
of the connected components
\Lambda^{n}_{k}
of
\partial{A}_{k}
lying strictly inside of
R
such that
m_{k}^{1}\leq m_{1}
.
Moreover, thanks to the fact that
{\mathcal{S}_{L}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)<\infty
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the family
\{\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\}_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}}
of connected components
\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}
that intersect
T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}
or
T_{2}^{\delta^{\prime}}
of
\partial{A}_{k}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
, respectively, are at most countable.
Furthermore, we define
\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}
for
i=1,2
by

\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}:=\left(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N},\,\Lambda^{\alpha}\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}\neq\emptyset}\Lambda^{\alpha}\right)\cup T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}

We denote by
\pi_{T}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
the orthogonal projection of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
onto
T
and since for every
n=1,\ldots,m_{k}+2
,
\Lambda^{n}
is a connected set we have that
\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{n})
is a homeomorphic to a closed interval in
\mathbb{R}
. More precisely,
\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{j})
is equal to a finite family of closed segments.
Thanks to the facts that
\overline{R}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T\cap\overline{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, and
m_{k}\leq m_{1}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{n})\right)=0

and hence, there exists
k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}>k_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that

(5.55)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{n})\right)<(m_{1}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
.
We denote by
a_{n},b_{n}\in T\cap\overline{R}
for every
n=1,\ldots,m_{k}+2
the initial and final point of each
\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{j})
, respectively (notice that
a_{m_{k}+1}\in T\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{1}
and
b_{m_{k}+2}\in T\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{2}
).
We decompose
\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{n})
as the finite union of disjoint open connected sets
\{C_{j}\}_{j\in J}
, where the endpoints of
C_{j}
are denoted by
a^{\prime}_{j},b^{\prime}_{j}\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{k}+2}\{a_{j},b_{j}\}
for every
j\in J
, and
\bigcup_{j\in J}\{a^{\prime}_{j},b^{\prime}_{j}\}=\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\{a_{n},b_{n}\}
. Therefore, by definition the cardinality of
J
is bounded by
2m_{k}+3
,

\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{T}(\Lambda^{n})=\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j},

and also by (
5.55
) we have that

(5.56)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}\right)<(m_{1}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
.
Let
T_{a^{\prime}_{j}}
and
T_{b^{\prime}_{j}}
be the lines parallel to
\nu_{T}
and passing through
a^{\prime}_{j}
and
b^{\prime}_{j}
, respectively. Finally, we denote by
S^{j}
the intersection of the strip between
T_{a^{\prime}_{j}}
and
T_{b^{\prime}_{j}}
and
R\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}
for every
j\in J
. If
j\in J
is such that
C_{j}\cap T^{\delta}_{1}
, we define
T^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}:=T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{1}
and
T^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{j}}=T_{b^{\prime}_{j}}\cap\overline{S^{j}}
, analogously, if
j\in J
is such that
C_{j}\cap T^{\delta}_{2}
we define
T^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}=T_{a^{\prime}_{j}}\cap\overline{S^{j}}
and
T^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{j}}:=T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{2}
, otherwise, if
C_{j}\cap\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}T^{\delta}_{i}=\emptyset
,
T^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}=T_{a^{\prime}_{j}}\cap\overline{S^{j}}
and
T^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{j}}=T_{b^{\prime}_{j}}\cap\overline{S^{j}}
.
It follows that

(5.57)

\mathcal{H}^{1}(T^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}\cup T^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{j}})\leq 2\delta^{\prime}.

From now on we fix
j\in J
and we consider a fixed
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}^{1}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})>0
. For simplicity in the following part of this step we denote
\Lambda^{n}:=\Lambda^{n}_{k}
for every
n=1,\ldots,m_{k}+2
.
By applying Lemma
5.5
(with
R
, as from the notation of Lemma
5.5
, coinciding with
S^{j}
) there exist
M_{k}:=M_{k}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},\delta^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
and
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
such
that
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}:=(\Gamma^{\mathrm{{A}}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}))\cap S^{j}
has
M_{k}
connected components and

(5.58)

{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},S^{j})-c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

and there exists a path
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}\subset\partial\widetilde{A}_{k}
such that
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}\cap T^{\prime}_{c_{j}}\neq\emptyset
and
\partial\widetilde{S}_{k}\cap\overline{S^{j}}\cap T^{\prime}_{c_{j}}\neq\emptyset
for
c_{j}\in\{a^{\prime}_{j},b^{\prime}_{j}\}
.
Let
\{\Gamma_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{M_{k}}
be the family of connected components of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
. Without loss of generality, we assume that
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{j}
intersects all islands and voids of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
that are not full ones (see Definition
5.6
), because otherwise we can always reduce to this situation by repeating Steps 3.1 and 3.2 of Lemma
5.7
.
If
M_{k}=0
, by repeating the same arguments of Step 1 we obtain that

(5.59)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{3}c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Therefore, by (
5.58
) and (
5.59
), we deduce that

(5.60)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-4c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

In the remaining of this step we assume that
M_{k}\geq 1
by proving the following claim: If
M_{k}\geq 1
,
then there exists
an island of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
or a substrate grain of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
(see Definition
5.6
). In order to prove this claim we proceed by contradiction.
Therefore, let us assume that
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})
does not contain any island and substrate grain. Since
M_{k}\geq 1
, there exists
\ell_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,M_{k}\}
and, since the endpoints of
\Gamma_{\ell_{1}}
must be connected through
\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}})
, then there exists an
open connected set
D_{1}\subset{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{A}_{k})
enclosed by
