\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}})
such that
\Gamma_{\ell_{1}}\subset\partial D_{1}
. By assumption, since
D_{1}
cannot be an island and cannot be a grain of
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})
, there must exist
\ell_{2}\in\{1,\ldots,M_{k}\}\setminus\{\ell_{1}\}
such that
\Gamma_{\ell_{2}}\subset\overline{D_{1}}
. Since the
endpoints of
\Gamma_{\ell_{2}}
must be connected through
\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}})
, then there exists an open connected set
D_{2}\subset{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{A}_{k})
enclosed by
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}

and
\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}})
such that
\Gamma_{\ell_{2}}\subset\partial D_{2}
, and we notice that
D_{2}
cannot coincide with
D_{1}
since
\partial D_{1}\cap(\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}))
is not connecting the endpoints of
\Gamma_{\ell_{2}}
(besides not connecting also the endpoints of
\Gamma_{\ell_{1}}
). By keeping on iterating this reasoning we reach a
contradiction with the fact that the family
M_{k}<\infty
, and hence the claim holds true.

Figure 5
.
The illustration represents the configuration of the admissible pair
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})
that is modified in Step 2 of Lemma
5.8
to obtain the pair
(\breve{A}_{k},\breve{S}_{k})
for which in the same step is proved the existence of at least a film island or a substrate grain in accordance with Definition
5.4
.

Step 3.
In this step we assume that
M_{k}\geq 1
(for the case
M_{k}=0
see Step 2) and we
prove that

(5.61)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-8c_{2}\delta^{\prime},

from which it easily follows from (
3.15
), (
5.57
) and (
5.58
),
that

(5.62)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-9c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

In order to prove (
5.61
) we consider an auxiliary energy
\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}
given by
\mathcal{S}_{L}
with an extra term, namely defined by

(5.63)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R):=\mathcal{S}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},R)\,+\sum_{c_{j}\in\{a^{\prime}_{j},b^{\prime}_{j}\}}2\int_{T^{\prime}_{c_{j}}}(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}

for every
(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k})\in\mathcal{B}
, and we prove that

(5.64)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},

since (
5.61
) directly follows from (
5.64
) by (
3.15
) and (
5.57
). To prove (
5.64
) we proceed by induction on the number
M_{k}\in\mathbb{N}

of connected components of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\text{A}}_{k}
in three steps.
In Steps 3.1 and 3.2 we show the basis of the induction for
M_{k}=1
, by considering the two cases provided by Step 2, i.e., the case in which
\widetilde{A}_{k}
presents an island in Step 3.1 and the case in which
\widetilde{S}_{k}
presents a substrate grain in Step 3.2. We conclude then the induction in Step 3.3.

Step 3.1

Assume that
M_{k}=1

and that there exists an island
P_{1}\subset\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{S}_{k})
of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
such that
P_{1}
is enclosed by
\Gamma_{1}\cup\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}))
with
\Gamma_{1}\subset\partial P_{1}
. Let
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
be the endpoints of
\Gamma_{1}
, and let
L_{1}
be the segment connecting
p_{1}
with
p_{2}
. We denote by
P_{1}^{1}
the open set enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\partial P_{1}\cap\Gamma_{1}
and we denote by
P_{1}^{2}
the open set enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\partial P_{1}\setminus\Gamma_{1}
.

We define a modification of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
, denoted by
\widehat{S}_{k}
, and a modification of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
, denoted by
\widehat{A}_{k}
, as

\widehat{S}_{k}:=(\widetilde{S}_{k}\setminus(P^{1}_{1}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{S}_{k})))\cup(P^{1}_{1}\cap(S^{j}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})),

and

\widehat{A}_{k}:=\widetilde{A}_{k}\setminus\left(P_{1}^{2}\cap({\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{A}_{k})\setminus\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}})\right)\cup\left(P_{1}^{2}\cap(S^{j}\setminus\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}})\right),

respectively. Notice that
(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k})\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma_{k}(\Omega))
.
By the anisotropic minimality of segments, it follows that

(5.65)

\begin{split}\int_{\partial P_{1}\setminus\widetilde{C}_{1}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{\partial P_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}+\int_{\partial P_{1}\cap\widetilde{C}_{1}}\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{\partial P_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}&\geq\int_{L_{1}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{L_{1}})+\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{L_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\int_{L_{1}}\phi(\nu_{L_{n}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1},\end{split}

where in the second inequality we used the definition of
\phi
, and hence, by (
5.65
) we obtain that

(5.66)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k},S^{j}).

Moreover, we observe that by construction
\widehat{\gamma}:=(\widetilde{\gamma}\setminus(\widetilde{\gamma}\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}})))\cup L_{1}
is path connected and it joins
T\cap T^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}
with
T\cap T^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{j}}
, and
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}})){\cap S^{j}}
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible. Thus, by repeating the same arguments of Step 1, we deduce that

(5.67)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

By (
5.57
), (
5.58
), (
5.63
), (
5.66
), and (
5.67
) we obtain (
5.61
).

Step 3.2

Assume that
M_{k}=1
and that there exists a substrate grain
G_{1}\subset\overline{\widetilde{S}_{k}}
of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
such that
G_{1}
is enclosed by
\Gamma_{1}\cup\widetilde{\gamma}\cup\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widetilde{A}_{k}}))
with
\Gamma_{1}\subset\partial G_{1}
. Let
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
be the endpoints of
\Gamma_{1}
, and let
L_{1}
be the segment connecting
p_{1}
with
p_{2}
. We denote by
G_{1}^{1}
the open set enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\partial G_{1}\cap\Gamma_{1}
and we denote by
G_{1}^{2}
the open set enclosed by
L_{1}
and
\partial G_{1}\setminus\Gamma_{1}
.

We define a modification of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
, denoted by
\widehat{S}_{k}
, and a modification of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
, denoted by
\widehat{A}_{k}
, as

\widehat{S}_{k}:=\widetilde{S}_{k}\setminus((G^{2}_{1}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{S}_{k}))\cup(G^{1}_{1}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{S}_{k}))),

and

\widehat{A}_{k}:=\widetilde{A}_{k}\setminus\left(G^{2}_{1}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{A}_{k})\right)\cup\left(G_{1}^{1}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\widetilde{A}_{k})\right),

respectively (see Figure
6
). Notice that
(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k})\in\mathcal{B}(\sigma_{k}(\Omega))
.

By the anisotropic minimality of segments, it follows that

(5.68)

\begin{split}\int_{\partial G_{1}\setminus\Gamma_{1}}\phi(\nu_{\partial G_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}+\int_{\partial G_{1}\cap\Gamma_{1}}\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{\partial G_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}&\geq\int_{L_{1}}\phi(\nu_{L_{1}})+\phi_{\text{FS}}(\nu_{L_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\int_{L_{1}}\phi_{\text{F}}(\nu_{L_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1},\end{split}

where in the second inequality we used (
3.16
) and hence, by (
5.68
) we obtain that

(5.69)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},\widetilde{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k},S^{j}).

Figure 6
.
The two illustrations above represent, passing from the left to the right, the construction that consists in “modifying grains in new voids”, which is contained in Step 3.2 of the proof of the Lemma
5.8
for the modification of the grain in a new void.

Moreover, we observe that by construction
\widehat{\gamma}:=(\widetilde{\gamma}\setminus(\widetilde{\gamma}\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\breve{A}_{k}})))\cup L_{1}
is path connected and it joins
T\cap T^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}_{j}}
with
T\cap T^{\prime}_{b^{\prime}_{j}}
, and
(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}})){\cap S^{j}}
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible. Thus, by repeating the same arguments of Step 1, we deduce that

(5.70)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

By (
3.15
), (
5.57
), (
5.58
), (
5.63
), (
5.69
), and (
5.70
) we obtain (
5.61
).

Step 3.3
. Assume that (
5.61
)
holds true if
M_{k}=i-1
. We need to show that (
5.61
) holds also if
M_{k}=i
. By Step 2 we have two cases:

(a)

i\geq 1
and there exists at least an island
P\subset S^{j}
of
\widetilde{A}_{k}
;

(b)

i\geq 1
and there exists at least a grain substrate
G\subset S^{j}
of
\widetilde{S}_{k}
.

In the case (a) we proceed by applying the same construction done in Step 3.1 with respect to the island
P
instead of
P_{1}
obtaining the configuration
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\in\mathcal{B}(\Omega_{k})
. We observe that by construction the set
\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}^{\text{A}}:=(\Gamma^{\mathrm{{A}}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}}))\cap S^{j}
has cardinality
i-1
(since the island
P
of
\breve{A}_{k}
is shrunk in
\widehat{A}_{k}
) and hence, we obtain that

(5.71)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

where we used the inductive hypothesis in the last inequality.

In the case (b) we proceed by applying the same construction done in Step 3.2 with respect to the substrate grain
G
instead of
G_{1}
obtaining the configuration
\left(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}}\right)\in\mathcal{B}
. We observe that by construction the set
\widehat{\Gamma}_{k}^{\text{A}}:=(\Gamma^{\mathrm{{A}}}_{\mathrm{FS}}(\widehat{A}_{k},\widehat{S}_{k})\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\overline{\widehat{S}_{k}}))\cap S^{j}
has cardinality
i-1
(since the grain
G
of
\breve{S}_{k}
is opened in a void) and hence, we obtain that

(5.72)

\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widetilde{A}_{k},{\widetilde{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\mathcal{S}^{1}_{L}(\widehat{A}_{k},{\widehat{S}_{k}},{R})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\text{F}}({\nu_{T}})+\phi({\nu_{T}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

where we used the inductive hypothesis in the last inequality.

Step 4.
Let
k\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
, which was defined in Step 2.
If
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})>0
, we consider
j\in J
.
By repeating the arguments of Step 1 in
S^{j}
if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})=0
(see (
5.54
)), and by Steps 2 and 3 (see (
5.60
) and (
5.61
)) we obtain that

(5.73)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}&(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{T})+\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-9c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.\end{split}

Therefore, in view of the fact that the cardinality of
J
is bounded by
2m_{k}+3
, by (
5.56
) and (
5.73
) it follows that

(5.74)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)&\geq\sum_{j\in J}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\\
&\geq\int_{\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{T})+\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}-9(2m_{k}+3)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{T})+\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-2(m_{1}+2)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}-9(2m_{k}+3)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{T})+\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(20m_{1}+31)c_{2}\delta^{\prime},\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used that
m_{k}\leq m_{1}
.
By recalling once again (
5.54
) of Step 1, we observe that by (
5.74
) we obtain that

(5.75)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)\geq\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{T})+\phi(\nu_{T})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(20m_{1}+31)c_{2}\delta^{\prime},\end{split}

for both the case with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})>0
and the case with
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})=0
. Finally, follows (
5.50
) from choosing
k_{\delta}:=k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{(20m_{1}+31)c_{2}}
for
\delta\in(0,\min\{(20m_{1}+31)c_{2},1\})
in (
5.75
). This completes the proof.
∎

We continue with the situation of the substrate cracks in the film-substrate incoherent interface.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
T_{0}
be the
x_{2}
-axis. Let
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1]
be such that
\rho_{k}\searrow 0
and
Q_{1}\subset\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega)
.
If
\{(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega))
is a sequence such that
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
and
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
small enough, there exists
k_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.76)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q_{1})\geq 2\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}}{\phi(\bm{e_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\delta

for any
k\geq k_{\delta}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we assume that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q_{1})<\infty
.
Since
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
and
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, for every
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
there exits
k_{\delta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.77)

\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus A_{k},\,\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\subset{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}

for every
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
, where
T^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in Q_{1}:\text{dist}(x,T_{0})<\delta^{\prime}/2\}
is the tubular neighborhood with thickness
\delta^{\prime}
of
T_{0}
in
Q_{1}
. Let
T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{1}
and
T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{2}
be the connected components of
\partial Q_{1}\cap\overline{T^{\delta^{\prime}}}
. Since
(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{k}(\Omega))
we can find an enumeration

\{\Gamma^{n}_{k}\}_{1,\ldots,m_{k}^{0}}

of connected components
\Gamma^{n}_{k}
of
\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}}
lying strictly inside of
Q_{1}
, and an enumeration
\{\Lambda^{n}_{k}\}_{1,\ldots,m_{k}^{1}}
of the connected components
\Lambda^{n}_{k}
of
\partial{A}_{k}
lying strictly inside of
Q_{1}
, such that
m_{k}^{\ell}\leq m_{\ell}
for
\ell=0,1
. Moreover,
thanks to the fact that
{\mathcal{S}_{L}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)<\infty
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
the families
\{\Gamma^{\alpha}_{k}\}_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\}_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}}
of connected components
\Gamma^{\alpha}_{k}
and
\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}
that intersect
T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}
or
T_{2}^{\delta^{\prime}}
of
\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
and of
\partial{A}_{k}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
, respectively, are at most countable. Furthermore, we define
\Gamma^{m_{k}+i}
and
\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}
for
i=1,2
by

\Gamma^{m_{k}+i}:=\left(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N},\,\Gamma^{\alpha}\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}\neq\emptyset}\Gamma^{\alpha}\right)\cup T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}:=\left(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N},\,\Lambda^{\alpha}\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}\neq\emptyset}\Lambda^{\alpha}\right)\cup T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}.

Thanks to the Kuratowski convergences of
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus A_{k}
and
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus S_{h_{k},K_{k}}
to
T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, the fact that
m^{\ell}_{k}\leq m_{\ell}
for
\ell=0,1
and for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{0}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Gamma^{n})\right)=0\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n})\right)=0

Hence, there exists
k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that

(5.78)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{0}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Gamma^{n})\right)<(m_{0}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
, and there exists
k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that

(5.79)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n})\right)<(m_{1}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Let
k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}:=\max\{k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}},k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}\}
. Similarly to Step 2 of Lemma
5.8
, we can decompose
\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Gamma^{n})
and
\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n})
as the finite union of disjoint open connected sets
\mathscr{C}^{0}:=\{C^{0}_{j}\}_{j\in J_{0}}
and
\mathscr{C}^{1}:=\{C^{1}_{j}\}_{j\in J_{1}}
, respectively. Notice that the cardinality of
J_{\ell}
is bounded by
2m_{k}^{\ell}+3
for
\ell=0,1
. Therefore

\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{0}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Gamma^{n})=\bigcup_{j\in J_{0}}C^{0}_{j},\quad\text{and}\quad\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n})=\bigcup_{j\in J_{1}}C^{1}_{j},

and also by (
5.78
) and (
5.79
) we have that

(5.80)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in J_{\ell}}C^{\ell}_{j}\right)<(m_{\ell}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
\ell=0,1
. We observe that

\mathscr{C}:=\{C:\text{$\emptyset\neq C=C^{0}\cap C^{1}$ with $C^{\ell}\in\mathscr{C}^{\ell}$ for $\ell=0,1$}\}

is a family of pairwise disjoint sets and has cardinality
m^{k}
bounded by
(2m_{k}^{0}+3)(2m_{k}^{1}+3)
, i.e.,
\mathscr{C}:=\{C_{j}\}_{j\in J}

for an index set
J
with cardinality
m^{k}\leq(2m_{k}^{0}+3)(2m_{k}^{1}+3)
. We observe that

(5.81)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}\right)\leq\sum_{\ell\in\{0,1\}}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in J_{\ell}}C^{\ell}_{j}\right)<(m_{0}+m_{1}+4)\delta^{\prime},

where we used (
5.80
) in the last inequality. Finally, let
S^{j}:=([-1,1]\times_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}C_{j})\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}
and let
T^{j}_{1}
and
T^{j}_{2}
the portions of the boundary of
S^{j}
parallels to the
x_{1}
-axis for every
j\in J
.

We now prove (
5.76
) in three steps. In the first step, we prove (
5.76
) for
k\geq k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that

\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}:=\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{\text{FS}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}}){\cap S^{j}}
is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible by repeating the same arguments of Step 1 in the proof of Lemma
5.8
. In the second step, by arguing as in Steps 2 and 3 in the proof of
5.8
we prove (
5.50
) for those
k\geq k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k})
is positive. In the last step, by arguing as in Step 4 in the proof of Lemma
5.8
we obtain (
5.76
).

Step 1.
Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\right)=0
for a fix
k\geq k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. By construction and by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
there exists a curve with support
\gamma_{1}\subset\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap(S^{j}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k},K_{k}}))
connecting
T^{j}_{1}
with
T^{j}_{2}
, and hence,
by
[
36
, Lemma 4.3]
, there exists also a curve with support
\gamma_{2}\subset\overline{\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\setminus(\gamma_{1}\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}})}
such that
\gamma_{1}\cap\partial^{*}S_{h_{k},K_{k}}
,
\gamma_{2}\cap\partial^{*}S_{h_{k},K_{k}}
are disjoint up to an
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible set and
\gamma_{2}
joins
T^{j}_{1}
with
T^{j}_{2}
. Since
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\right)=0
, it yields that

(5.82)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})&+\sum_{i=1}^{2}2\int_{T^{j}_{i}}\phi(\nu_{T^{j}_{i}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\int_{(\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{2})\cap\partial^{*}S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k}}}{\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{2})\cap(\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cap\partial{A_{k}})\cap(S^{(1)}_{h_{k},K_{k}}\cup A^{(0)}_{k})}{2\phi(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\gamma_{1}\cup\gamma_{2})\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial A_{k}\cap{A_{k}}^{(1)}}{(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{2}2\int_{T^{j}_{i}}\phi(\nu_{T^{j}_{i}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&=\int_{{\Gamma_{1}}}{\phi(\nu_{{\Gamma_{1}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\Gamma_{2}}{\phi(\nu_{\Gamma_{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},\end{split}

where
\Gamma_{1}:=T^{j}_{1}\cup{\gamma_{1}}\cup T^{j}_{2}
and
\Gamma_{2}:=T^{j}_{1}\cup{\gamma_{2}}\cup T^{j}_{2}
. From the anisotropic minimality of segments (see
[
43
, Remark 20.3]
) it follows that

(5.83)

\int_{{\Gamma_{1}}}{\phi(\nu_{{\Gamma_{1}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\int_{\Gamma_{2}}{\phi(\nu_{\Gamma_{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{{\phi}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}},

and so, thanks to the facts that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(T^{j}_{1}\cup T^{j}_{2})\leq 2\delta^{\prime}
, and by (
3.15
), (
5.77
), (
5.82
) and (
5.83
), we deduce that

(5.84)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{{\phi}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{4}c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Step 2.
Assume that
\mathcal{H}^{1}\left(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\right)>0
for a fix
k\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
. By construction and by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]
, there exists a curve with support
\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}\subset\partial A_{k}
connecting
T^{j_{1}}_{1}
with
T^{j_{1}}_{2}
. If
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}\cap(\partial A_{k}\setminus\partial S_{h_{k}}))=0
, by repeating the same arguments of Step 1, we can deduce that

(5.85)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{{\phi}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-{4}c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

If
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{1}\cap(\partial A_{k}\setminus\partial S_{h_{k}}))>0
, then by reasoning as it was done in the proof of Lemma
5.8
to reach (
5.62
) we obtain

(5.86)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{\phi(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-9c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

More precisely, we reach (
5.86
) by noticing that
\nu_{T_{0}}=\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
and by following the proof of Step 2 (from after equation (
5.57
)) and of Step 3 in Lemma
5.8
with the only difference that we replace the reference to Step 1 of Lemma
5.8
with the reference to Step 1 of the current lemma and the extra term appearing in (
5.63
) with

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{T^{j}_{i}}(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+3\phi)(\bm{e_{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}.

.

Step 3.
Let
k\geq k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and let
j\in J
. By applying (
5.84
) if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})=0
and (
5.86
) if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})>0
we obtain that

(5.87)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},\widetilde{S}^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{{\phi}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-9c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Therefore, since the cardinality of
J
is bounded by
(2m_{k}^{0}+3)\cdot(2m_{k}^{1}+3)
, (
5.87
) yields

(5.88)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}&(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q_{1})\geq\sum_{j\in J}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\\
&\geq 2\int_{\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}}\phi(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}-9(2m_{k}^{0}+3)\cdot(2m_{k}^{1}+3)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&\geq 2\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}}{\phi(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(m_{0}+m_{1}+4)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}-9(2m_{k}^{0}+3)\cdot(2m_{k}^{1}+3)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&=2\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\alpha\delta^{\prime},\end{split}

where
\alpha:=(m_{0}+m_{1}+4)c_{2}+9(2m_{0}+3)\cdot(2m_{1}+3)
and in the last inequality we used that
m^{\ell}_{k}\leq m_{\ell}
for
\ell=0,1
.
Finally, (
5.76
) follows from choosing
k_{\delta}:=k^{3}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{\alpha}
for
\delta\in(0,\min\{\alpha,1\})
in (
5.88
). This completes the proof.
∎

We continue by treating the situation related to the blow up at a point in the filaments of both the substrate and the film.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
T_{0}
be the
x_{2}
-axis. Let
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1]
be such that
\rho_{k}\searrow 0
and
Q_{1}\subset\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega)
.
If
\{(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega))
is a sequence such that

Q_{1}\cap A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
Q_{1}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
small enough, there exists
k_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.89)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},h_{k},K_{k},Q_{1})\geq 2\int_{T_{0}}{\phi^{\prime}(\bm{e_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\delta

for any
k\geq k_{\delta}
.

Proof.

The situation is symmetric to the situation of Lemma
5.9
and the proof is the same since
\phi^{\prime}\leq\phi
. ∎

We continue with the situation in the blow up of the substrate filaments on the film free boundary.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
T_{0}
be the
x_{2}
-axis. Let
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1]
be such that
\rho_{k}\searrow 0
and
Q\subset\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega)\cap H_{0,-\bm{e_{2}}}
be an open square whose sides are either parallel or perpendicular to
\bm{e_{1}}
and
T_{0}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
.
If
\{(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega))
is a sequence such that
\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}
and
\overline{Q}\cap A_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}H_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}}\cap\overline{Q}
, then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
k_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
k\geq k_{\delta}
,

(5.90)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)\geq\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}({\bm{e}}_{{\bm{1}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\delta.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we assume that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)<\infty
.
Since
\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q_{1}}
and
\overline{Q}\cap A_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}H_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}}\cap\overline{Q}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
for every
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
there exists
k_{\delta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.91)

\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}},\,Q\cap\partial A_{k}\subset T^{\delta^{\prime}},

where
T^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in Q:{\rm dist}(x,T_{0})<\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}\}
.
Let
T_{1}
be the upper side of
Q
and let
T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in Q:\text{dist}(x,T_{1})<\delta^{\prime}/2\}
. By the Kuratowski convergence of
\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}
, there exists
k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that
S_{h_{k}}\cap\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}}\neq\emptyset
for every
k\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Let
R:=(T^{\delta^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}})
and let
T^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime}_{2}\subset\partial R
be the upper and lower side of the rectangle
R
, respectively.

Since
(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{k}(\Omega))
we can find an enumeration
\{\Lambda^{n}_{k}\}_{n=1,\ldots,m_{k}^{1}}
of the connected components
\Lambda^{n}_{k}
of
\partial{A}_{k}
lying strictly inside of
R
, such that
m_{k}^{1}\leq m_{1}
. Moreover, thanks to the fact that
{\mathcal{S}_{L}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)<\infty
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the family
\{\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\}_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}}
of connected components
\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}
of
\partial{A}_{k}\cap\overline{R}
that intersect
T_{1}^{\prime}
or
T_{2}^{\prime}
, respectively, are at most countable. Furthermore, we define
\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}
for
i=1,2
by

\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}_{k}:=\left(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N},\,\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}\neq\emptyset}\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\right)\cup T^{\prime}_{i}.

Thanks to the Kuratowski convergences of
\overline{R}\cap\partial A_{k}
to
T_{0}\cap\overline{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, the fact that
m^{1}_{k}\leq m_{1}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})\right)=0

Hence, there exists
k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that

(5.92)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})\right)<(m_{1}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Similarly to Step 2 of Lemma
5.8
, we can decompose
\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})
as the finite union of disjoint open connected sets
\mathscr{C}:=\{C_{j}\}_{j\in J}
. Notice that the cardinality of
J
is bounded by
2m_{k}^{1}+3
. Therefore

\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})=\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j},

and also by (
5.104
) we have that

(5.93)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}\right)<(m_{1}+2)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Finally, let
S^{j}:=(\pi_{1}(R)\times_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}C_{j})\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}
and let
T^{j}_{1}
and
T^{j}_{2}
be the upper and lower sides of the boundary of each rectangle
S^{j}
, respectively.

Step 1.
Let
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
j\in J
be such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{FS}}^{\text{A}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\cap S^{j})=0
.
In view of the construction of
S^{j}
, by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]

there exists a curve with support
\gamma_{k}\subset\partial(A_{k}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k}}))
joining
T^{j}_{1}
with
T^{j}_{2}
. It follows that

(5.94)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)&+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{T^{j}_{i}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\int_{\gamma_{k}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\setminus\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{\gamma_{k}\cap(\partial A_{k}\setminus\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\cap(A_{k}^{(0)}\cup A_{k}^{(1)})}{2\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{\gamma_{k}\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial A_{k}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}}{(\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{\gamma_{k}\cap\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}^{(0)}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{T^{j}_{i}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\int_{\widehat{\gamma}_{k}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1},\end{split}

where
\widehat{\gamma}_{k}:=T^{j}_{1}\cup\gamma_{k}\cup T^{j}_{2}
.
By the anisotropic minimality of segments, we deduce that

(5.95)

\begin{split}\int_{\widehat{\gamma}_{k}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}&\geq\int_{C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}.\end{split}

By (
3.15
), (
5.94
) and (
5.95
), we conclude that

(5.96)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}-2c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

Step 2.
Let
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
j\in J
be such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{FS}}^{\text{A}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\cap S^{j})>0
. By reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
5.8
to reach (
5.62
) we obtain

(5.97)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-7c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

More precisely, we reach (
5.97
) by noticing that
\nu_{T_{0}}=\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
and by following the proof of Step 2 (from after equation (
5.57
)) and of Step 3 of Lemma
5.8
with the only difference that we replace the reference to Step 1 of Lemma
5.8
with the reference to Step 1 of the current lemma, and the extra term appearing in (
5.63
) with

(5.98)

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{T^{j}_{i}}(2\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\bm{e_{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}.

Notice that the difference of two units more in the error term of (
5.97
) with respect to (
5.62
) is due to the fact that the extra term (
5.98
) contributes to the final error with exactly two units more.

Step 3.
Fix
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and let
j\in J
. By (
5.96
) if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})=0
and by (
5.97
) if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})>0
we obtain that

(5.99)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{k},S^{j})\geq\int_{C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}-7c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

Therefore, since the cardinality of
J
is bounded by
2(m_{1}+3)
, (
5.99
) yields

(5.100)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)&\geq\sum_{j\in J}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq\int_{\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}-7(2m_{1}+3)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&\geq\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(m_{1}+2)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}-7(2m_{1}+3)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&=\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(15m_{1}+24)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.\end{split}

By the non-negativeness of
\phi_{\mathrm{F}},\phi
and
\phi_{\mathrm{FS}}
we have that

(5.101)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)&\geq\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)\geq\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(15m_{1}+24)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&\geq\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(15m_{1}+25)c_{2}\delta^{\prime},\end{split}

where in the second inequality we used (
5.101
) and in the last inequality we added and subtracted
\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\bm{e_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}
, and we used (
3.15
) and the fact that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(T_{0}\cap\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}})\leq\delta^{\prime}/2
. Finally, (
5.90
) follows from choosing
k_{\delta}:=k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{(15m_{1}+25)c_{2}}
for
\delta\in(0,\min\{(15m_{1}+25)c_{2},1\})
in (
5.111
). This completes the proof. ∎

We conclude these list of estimates by addressing the setting of the delaminated substrate filaments in the film.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
T_{0}
be the
x_{2}
-axis. Let
\{\rho_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1]
be such that
\rho_{k}\searrow 0
and
Q\subset\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega)\cap H_{0,-\bm{e_{2}}}
be an open square whose sides are either parallel or perpendicular to
\bm{e_{1}}
and
T_{0}\cap Q\neq\emptyset
.
If
\{(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{1}}(\Omega))
is a sequence such that
\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}
and
\overline{Q}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}
, then for every
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
k_{\delta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
k\geq k_{\delta}
,

(5.102)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)\geq\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}}{2\phi_{\mathrm{F}}({\bm{e}}_{{\bm{1}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\delta.

Proof.

Without loss of generality we assume that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)<\infty
. Since
\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}
and
\overline{Q}\setminus A_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}\cap\overline{Q}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
for every
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
there exists
k_{\delta^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(5.103)

\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}},\,\overline{Q}\setminus A_{k}\subset T^{\delta^{\prime}},

where
T^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in Q:{\rm dist}(x,T_{0})<\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{2}\}
. Let
T_{1}
be the upper side of
Q
and let
T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}:=\{x\in Q:\text{dist}(x,T_{1})<\delta^{\prime}/2\}
. By the Kuratowski convergence of
\overline{Q}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}
, there exists
k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}\geq k_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that
S_{h_{k}}\cap\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}}\neq\emptyset
for every
k\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Let
R:=(T^{\delta^{\prime}}\setminus\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}})
and let
T^{\prime}_{1},T^{\prime}_{2}\subset\partial R
be the upper and lower side of the rectangle
R
, respectively.

Since
(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{k}(\Omega))
we can find an enumeration
\{\Lambda^{n}_{k}\}_{n=1,\ldots,m_{k}^{1}}
of the connected components
\Lambda^{n}_{k}
of
\partial{A}_{k}
lying strictly inside of
R
, such that
m_{k}^{1}\leq m_{1}
. Moreover, thanks to the fact that
{\mathcal{S}_{L}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)<\infty
for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the family
\{\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\}_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}}
of connected components
\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}
of
\partial{A}_{k}\cap\overline{R}
that intersect
T_{1}^{\prime}
or
T_{2}^{\prime}
, respectively, are at most countable. Furthermore, we define
\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}
for
i=1,2
by

\Lambda^{m_{k}+i}_{k}:=\left(\bigcup_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N},\,\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}_{i}\neq\emptyset}\Lambda^{\alpha}_{k}\right)\cup T^{\prime}_{i}.

Thanks to the Kuratowski convergences of
\overline{Q}\setminus A_{k}
to
T_{0}\cap\overline{R}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as
k\to\infty
, the fact that
m^{1}_{k}\leq m_{1}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})\right)=0

Hence, there exists
k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}\geq k^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}
such that

(5.104)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})\right)<(m_{1}+2)\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Similarly to Step 2 of Lemma
5.8
, we can decompose
\bigcup_{n=1}^{m_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})
as the finite union of disjoint open connected sets
\mathscr{C}:=\{C_{j}\}_{j\in J}
. Notice that the cardinality of
J
is bounded by
2m_{k}^{1}+3
. Therefore

\bigcup_{n=1}^{m^{1}_{k}+2}\pi_{2}(\Lambda^{n}_{k})=\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j},

and also by (
5.104
) we have that

(5.105)

\mathcal{H}^{1}\left((T_{0}\cap\overline{R})\setminus\bigcup_{j\in J}C_{j}\right)<(m_{1}+2)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}

for every
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
. Finally, let
S^{j}:=(\pi_{1}(R)\times_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}C_{j})\cap T^{\delta^{\prime}}
and let
T^{j}_{1}
and
T^{j}_{2}
be the upper and lower sides of the boundary of each rectangle
S^{j}
, respectively.

Step 1.
Let
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
j\in J
be such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{FS}}^{\text{A}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\cap S^{j})=0
. In view of the construction of
S^{j}
, by
[
25
, Lemma 3.12]

we can find a curve with support
\gamma_{k}^{1}\subset\partial(A_{k}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k}}))
joining
T^{j}_{1}
with
T^{j}_{2}
, and there exists only one connected component of

\partial(A_{k}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k}}))
in
S^{j}
. Therefore, by applying
[
36
, Lemma 4.3]
there exists a curve connecting
T^{j}_{1}
and
T^{j}_{2}
with support
\gamma_{k}^{2}\subset S^{j}\cap\overline{\partial(A_{k}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k}}))\setminus(\gamma_{k}^{1}\cap\partial{\rm{Int}}(\overline{A_{k}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(S_{h_{k}})}))}
. It follows that

(5.106)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})&+2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{T_{i}^{\prime}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\int_{(\gamma^{1}_{k}\cup\gamma^{2}_{k})\cap(\partial^{*}A_{k}\setminus\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}})}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\gamma^{1}_{k}\cup\gamma^{2}_{k})\cap(\partial A_{k}\setminus\partial S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\cap A_{k}^{(0)}}{2\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\gamma^{1}_{k}\cup\gamma^{2}_{k})\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial A_{k}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{(\gamma^{1}_{k}\cup\gamma^{2}_{k})\cap\partial{S_{h_{k},K_{k}}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap S_{h_{k},K_{k}}^{(0)}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}+2\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{T_{i}^{\prime}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\\
&\geq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}^{i}_{k}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{\gamma}^{i}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1},\end{split}

where
\widetilde{\gamma}^{i}_{k}:=T^{j}_{1}\cup\gamma_{k}^{i}\cup T^{j}_{2}
for
i=1,2
. By the anisotropic minimality of segments, we deduce that

(5.107)

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}^{i}_{k}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\nu_{\widetilde{\gamma}^{i}_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}&\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}.\end{split}

By (
3.15
), (
5.106
) and (
5.107
), we conclude that

(5.108)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}-4c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

Step 2.
Let
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
j\in J
be such that
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma_{\mathrm{FS}}^{\text{A}}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\cap S^{j})>0
. By reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
5.8
to reach (
5.62
) we obtain

(5.109)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-9c_{2}\delta^{\prime}.

More precisely, we reach (
5.109
) by noticing that
\nu_{T_{0}}=\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
and by following the proof of Step 2 (from after equation (
5.57
)) and of Step 3 of Lemma
5.8
with the only difference that we replace the reference to Step 1 of Lemma
5.8
with the reference to Step 1 of the current lemma, and the extra term appearing in (
5.63
) with

\sum_{i=1,2}\int_{T^{j}_{i}}(3\phi_{\mathrm{F}}+\phi)(\bm{e_{2}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}.

Step 3.
Fix
k\geq k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and let
j\in J
. By (
5.108
) if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})=0
and by (
5.109
) if
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Gamma^{\text{A}}_{k}\cap S^{j})>0
we obtain that

(5.110)

\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},S^{j})\geq 2\int_{C_{j}}{{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-9c_{2}{\delta^{\prime}}.

Therefore, the same reasoning of Step 3 of Lemma
5.11
yields that

(5.111)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},Q)&\geq\mathcal{S}_{L}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},R)\geq 2\int_{T\cap\overline{R}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(35m_{1}+56)c_{2}\delta^{\prime}\\
&\geq 2\int_{T\cap\overline{Q}}{\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-(35m_{1}+57)c_{2}\delta^{\prime},\end{split}

where as a difference with Step 3 of Lemma
5.11
we added and subtracted
2\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{T_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}}}\phi_{\mathrm{F}}(\bm{e_{1}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}
in the last inequality. Finally, (
5.102
) follows from choosing
k_{\delta}:=k^{2}_{\delta^{\prime}}
and
\delta^{\prime}=\frac{\delta}{(35m_{1}+57)c_{2}}
for
\delta\in(0,\min\{(35m_{1}+57)c_{2},1\})
in (
5.111
). This completes the proof. ∎

We are now in the position to prove that the surface energy
\mathcal{S}
is lower semicontinuous in
\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}
with respect to the
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence.

Theorem 5.13

(Lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{S}
)
.

Let
(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{m}}
and
(A,S_{h,K})\in\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{m}}
such that
(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\mathcal{B}}}(A,S_{h,K})
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Then,

(5.112)

\mathcal{S}(A,S_{h,K})\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathcal{S}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that the
liminf
in the right side of (
5.112
) is reached and finite in
\mathbb{R}
. For every
k\in\mathbb{N}
we denote
S_{k}:=S_{h_{k},K_{k}}\in{\rm AS}(\Omega)
for simplicity and we define
\mu_{k}
as the Radon measure associated to
{\mathcal{S}}(A_{k},S_{k})
, i.e., the measure
\mu_{k}
given by

\mu_{k}(B):=\int_{B\cap\Omega\cap(\partial A_{k}\cup\partial S_{k})}{\psi_{k}(x,\nu_{k}\left(x\right))\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

for every Borel set
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
,
where
\nu_{k}:=\nu_{\partial A_{k}\cup\partial S_{k}}
and the surface tension
\psi_{k}
is defined by

(5.113)

\psi_{k}(x,\nu_{k}(x)):=\begin{cases}\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))&\text{if }x\in\partial^{*}A_{k}\setminus\partial{S_{k}}\\
\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))&\text{if }x\in\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap\partial^{*}{S_{k}},\\
{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))}&\text{if }x\in(\partial A_{k}\setminus\partial{S_{k}})\cap A^{(1)},\\
{2\varphi^{\prime}(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))}&\text{if }x\in(\partial A_{k}\setminus\partial{S_{k}})\cap A^{(0)},\\
\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(x,\nu_{S_{k}}(x))&\text{if }x\in(\partial^{*}{S_{k}}\setminus\partial A_{k})\cap A_{k}^{(1)},\\
{2\varphi(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))}&\text{if }x\in(\partial{S_{k}}\cap\partial A_{k})\cap S^{(1)}_{k},\\
{2\varphi^{\prime}(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))}&\text{if }x\in(\partial{S_{k}}\cap\partial A_{k})\cap A^{(0)},\\
{\phi(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))}&\text{if }x\in\partial{S_{k}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k}\cap S^{(0)}_{k},\\
2\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(x,\nu_{S_{k}}(x))&\text{if }x\in(\partial{S_{k}}\setminus\partial A_{k})\cap(S^{(1)}_{k}\cup S^{(0)}_{k})\cap A_{k}^{(1)},\\
(\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}+\varphi)(x,\nu_{A_{k}}(x))&\text{if }x\in\partial A_{k}\cap\partial^{*}{S_{k}}\cap A^{(1)},\\
{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(x,\nu_{S_{k}}(x))}&\text{if }x\in(\partial{S_{k}}\cap\partial A_{k})\cap S^{(0)}_{k}\cap A_{k}^{(1)}.\end{cases}

Furthermore, we denote by
\mu
the Radon measure associated to
{\mathcal{S}}(A,h,K)
, i.e., the measure
\mu
given by

\mu(B):=\int_{B\cap\Omega\cap(\partial A\cup\partial S_{h,K})}{\psi(x,\nu_{\partial A\cup\partial S_{h,K}}\left(x\right))\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

for every Borel set
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where
\psi
is defined analogously to
\psi_{k}
in (
5.113
), but with the sets
A_{k}
and
S_{k}
replaced with
A
and
S_{h,K}
, respectively.

We observe that by (H1) there exists
c:=c(c_{2})>0
such that

\mu_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{2})=\mathcal{S}(A_{k},S_{k})\leq c\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial{A_{k}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial{S_{k}})\right)

and since
(A_{k},S_{k})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\mathcal{B}}}(A,S_{h,K})
, we obtain that
\sup_{k}{\mu_{k}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}<+\infty
. It follows that
\{\mu_{k}\}
is a sequence of bounded Radon measures and hence, owing to the weak* compactness of Radon measures (see
[
43
, Theorem 4.33]
), there exist a not relabeled subsequence
\{\mu_{k}\}
and a Radon measure
\mu_{0}
such that
\mu_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}\mu_{0}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
.
The purpose of this proof is to show the following inequality in the sense of measures

(5.114)

\mu_{0}\geq\mu.

Since
\mu_{0}
and
\mu
are non-negative measures and
\mu<<\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial A\cup\partial{S_{h,K}})
, to obtain (
5.114
),
it is enough to prove that the surface tension
\psi
of
\mu
on each subset of
\partial A\cup\partial{S_{h,K}}
on which it is uniquely defined, is bounded from above by the Radon-Nikodym
derivative of
\mu_{0}
with respect to the
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-measure of the corresponding subset, namely the following 12 inequalities:

(5.115)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}})}\left(x\right)\geq\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))

\displaystyle\textrm{ for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. $x\in\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}$},

(5.116)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A)}\left(x\right)\geq\varphi(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))

for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e.
x\in\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A
,

(5.117)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)\geq 2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))

for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e.
x\in(\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)},

(5.118)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(0)})}\left(x\right)\geq{2\varphi^{\prime}(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))}

for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e.
x\in(\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(0)},

(5.119)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)\geq\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(x,\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right))\\
\textrm{ for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$- a.e. $x\in(\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap A^{(1)},$ }

(5.120)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K})}\left(x\right)\geq 2\varphi(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))

for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e.
x\in(\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K},

(5.121)

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap A^{(0)})}\left(x\right)\geq{2\varphi^{\prime}(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))}

for
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-a.e.
x\in(\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap A^{(0)},

(5.122)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial^{*}A\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K})}\left(x\right)\geq{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}}(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))\\
\textrm{for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. $x\in\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial^{*}A\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K},$}

(5.123)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)\geq 2\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(x,\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right))\\
\textrm{for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. }x\in(\partial{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)},

(5.124)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)\geq 2\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(x,\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right))\,\\
\textrm{for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. $x\in(\partial{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)},$}

(5.125)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)\geq(\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}+\varphi)(x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right))\\
\textrm{for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. $x\in\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)}$}

and

(5.126)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)\geq{{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}}}(x,\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right))\\
\textrm{for $\mathcal{H}^{1}$-a.e. $x\in(\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)}.$ }

The rest of the proof is devoted to establish the previous 12 lower-bound estimates. In order to do that we fix
\varepsilon>0
small enough and we recall that from the uniform continuity of
\varphi_{\mathrm{F}},\varphi_{\mathrm{S}},\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}
it follows that there exists a
\delta_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

(5.127)

\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\xi)\geq\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(x_{0},\xi)-\varepsilon,\quad\varphi_{\mathrm{S}}(y,\xi)\geq\varphi_{\mathrm{S}}(x_{0},\xi)-\varepsilon,\text{ and }\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(y,\xi)\geq\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(x_{0},\xi)-\varepsilon,

for every
y\in Q_{\delta_{\varepsilon}}(x_{0})\subset\Omega
,
x_{0}\in\Omega
and
\absolutevalue{\xi}=1
. The proofs of (
5.115
) and (
5.119
) are based on
[
43
, Theorem 20.1]
, the proofs of (
5.116
), (
5.120
), (
5.121
),
(
5.122
), (
5.125
) and (
5.126
) are based on Lemmas
5.7
,
5.8
,

5.9
,
5.10
,

5.11
and
5.12
, respectively. Finally, the proofs of (
5.118
) and (
5.124
) are based on
[
36
, Lemma 4.4]
, and the proofs of (
5.117
) and (
5.123
) are based on
[
36
, Lemma 4.5]
(See Table
1
).

Assertions

\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}

\nu_{A_{k}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}A_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}\nu_{A}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}A)

\varphi_{\text{F}}

[
43
, Theorem 20.1]

\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}{A}

\varphi

Lemma

[

, Lemma 4.5]

, Lemma 4.4]

\overline{Q_{1}}\cap S_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}}\end{matrix}

{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}}

Lemma
5.11

(\partial{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)}

\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus S_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}}

2\varphi_{\text{FS}}

[
36
, Lemma 4.5]

(\partial{S_{h,K}}\setminus\partial A)\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)}

\overline{Q_{1}}\cap S_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}}

2\varphi_{\text{FS}}

[
36
, Lemma 4.4]

\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)}

\begin{matrix}\overline{R_{\nu_{S_{h,K}}}}\cap S_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{R_{\nu_{S_{h,K}}}}\cap H_{0,\nu_{S_{h,K}}},\\
\overline{R_{\nu_{S_{h,K}}}}\setminus A_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{R_{\nu_{S_{h,K}}}}\cap T_{0,\nu_{S_{h,K}}}\end{matrix}
