Lemma
5.8

(\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(0)}_{h,K}\cap A^{(1)}

\begin{matrix}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap S_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}},\\
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus A_{k_{n}}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}}\end{matrix}

{2\varphi_{\text{F}}}

Lemma
5.12

Table 1
.
Sketch of the proof of (
5.115
)–(
5.126
) for Theorem
5.13
: in the blow-ups centered at a point of the sets listed in the first column, the corresponding conditions listed in the second column are proven to hold and the lower bounds of the localized surface energy are reached with surface tensions given in the third column by means of the assertions listed in the fourth column. Note that
R_{\nu_{U}}
for
U=A,S_{h,K}
is defined as
R_{\nu_{U}}:=Q_{1}
if
\nu_{U}=\textbf{e}_{i}
for
i=\textbf{1},\textbf{2}
, or, otherwise,
R_{\nu_{U}}:=(-\cos\theta_{\nu_{U}},\cos\theta_{\nu_{U}})\times_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(-\sin\theta_{\nu_{U}},\sin\theta_{\nu_{U}})
, where
\theta_{\nu_{U}}
is the angle formed between
T_{0,\nu_{U}}
and the
x_{1}
-axis.

Proof of (
5.115
).

We begin by observing that by the definition of
\partial^{*}A
, the continuity of
\varphi
, the Borel regularity of
y\in\partial^{*}A\mapsto\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A}(y))
and the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem (see
[
28
, Theorem 1.153]
), the set of points in
\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}
not satisfying the following 3 conditions:

(a1)

\nu_{A}\left(x\right)
exists,

(a2)

x
is a Lebesgue point of
y\in\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}\mapsto\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A}(y)),
i.e.,

\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}{\frac{1}{2r}{\int_{Q_{r}\cap\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}}{\absolutevalue{\varphi_{\mathrm{ F}}(y, \nu_A (y)) - \varphi_{\mathrm{ F}}(x, \nu_A \xp)}\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}(y)}}}=0,

(a3)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=4.82224pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.39182pt\vrule height=0.39182pt,depth=0.0pt,width=3.91806pt}(\partial^{*}{A}\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})}\left(x\right)
exists and it is finite,

is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible. Therefore, we prove (
5.115
) for a fixed
x\in\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}
satisfying (a1)-(a3). Without loss of generality, we consider
x=0
and
\nu_{A}(0)=\mathbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
, where we used (a1).

By
[
36
, Lemma 3.2-(b)]
and
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence we have that
A_{k}\rightarrow A
and
S_{k}\rightarrow S
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and hence,
D\mathbbm{1}_{A_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}D\mathbbm{1}_{A}
and
D\mathbbm{1}_{S_{k}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}D\mathbbm{1}_{S}
, hence by the Structure Theorem for sets of finite perimeter (see
[
24
, Theorem 5.15]
), it holds that

(5.128)

\nu_{A_{k}}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}A_{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ast}}{{\rightharpoonup}}\nu_{A}\mathcal{H}^{1}(\partial^{*}A).

Furthermore, by Remark (
3.8
)-(i) and again the the
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence we obtain that
\partial S_{k}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\partial S_{h,K}
, from which it follows that for any
\eta>0
there exists
k_{\eta}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\partial S_{k}\subset S^{\eta}
for every
k\geq k_{\eta}
, where
S^{\eta}:=\{x\in\overline{\Omega}:\,{\rm dist}(x,\partial S_{h,K})\leq\eta\}.

We observe that from the properties of Radon measures there exists a sequence
\rho_{n}\searrow 0
such that
Q_{\rho_{n}}\subset\subset\Omega
,
\mu_{0}(\partial Q_{\rho_{n}})=0
,

(5.129)

\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})=\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mu_{k}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})}

and

(5.130)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}{A}\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})}(0)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\frac{\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}},

where we also used (a3) and Besicovitch Derivation Theorem (see, e.g.,
[
28
, Theorem 1.153]
). Therefore, by (
5.129
) we deduce that

(5.131)

\begin{split}\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})&=\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mu_{k}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})}\geq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k}}\setminus{\partial{S_{k}}}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}\\
&\geq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k}}\setminus S^{\eta}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A_{k}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}\geq{\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A}\setminus S^{\eta}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used the non-negativeness of
\psi_{k}
, in the second inequality we used the fact that
\partial S_{k}\subset S^{\eta}
for every
k\geq k_{\eta}
and in the last inequality, by using the fact that
A_{k}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and (
5.128
), we apply (see, e.g.,
[
43
, Theorem 20.1]
). Moreover, taking
\eta\to 0
in (
5.131
) we obtain that

(5.132)

\begin{split}\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})&\geq\lim_{\eta\to 0}{\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A}\setminus S^{\eta}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}={\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A}\setminus\partial S_{h,K}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}\end{split}

by Lebesgue monotone convergence theorem
[
28
, Theorem 1.79]
.
Finally, by (
5.130
) and (a2) we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}})}(0)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\frac{\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\geq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\frac{1}{2\rho_{n}}\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A}\setminus{\partial{S_{h,K}}}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(y,\nu_{A})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\mathbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}).

∎

Proof of (
5.116
).

By the definition of
\partial^{*}A
and
\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}
, by
[
36
, Proposition A.4]
(applied with
K
taken as first
\partial A
and then
\partial{S_{h,K}}
), and by the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem the set of points
x\in\partial^{*}A\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}
not satisfying the following 3 conditions:

(b1)

\nu_{A}\left(x\right)
,
\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right)
exist and, either
\nu_{A}\left(x\right)=\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right)
or
\nu_{A}\left(x\right)=-\nu_{S_{h,K}}\left(x\right)
,

(b2)

for every open rectangle
R
containing
x
with sides parallel or perpendicular to
\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
we have that
\overline{R}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(A)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{R}\cap T_{x,\nu_{A}(x)}
and
\overline{R}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}({S_{h,K}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{R}\cap T_{x,\nu_{A}(x)}
as
\rho\to 0
, where
T_{x,\nu_{A}(x)}
is the approximate tangent line at
x
of
\partial A
(or of
\partial{S_{h,K}}
),

(b3)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=4.82224pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.39182pt\vrule height=0.39182pt,depth=0.0pt,width=3.91806pt}(\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A)}\left(x\right)
exists and it is finite,

is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible. Therefore, we prove (
5.116
) for any fixed
x\in\partial^{*}A\cap\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}
satisfying (b1)-(b3). Without loss of generality we assume that
x=0
and we denote
T_{0}=T_{0,\nu_{A}(0)}
. Furthermore, by using (b1) we choose in (b2) the rectangle
R_{\nu_{A}}:=Q_{1}
if
\nu_{A}(0)=\textbf{e}_{i}
for
i=\textbf{1},\textbf{2}
, or
R_{\nu_{A}}:=(-\cos\theta_{\nu_{A}},\cos\theta_{\nu_{A}})\times_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(-\sin\theta_{\nu_{A}},\sin\theta_{\nu_{A}})
, where
\theta_{\nu_{A}}
is the angle formed between the tangent line
T_{0}
and the
x_{1}
-axis, otherwise. For any
\rho>0
, we write
R_{\rho}:=\rho R_{\nu_{A}}
.

In view of the definition of
R_{\nu_{A}}
and again by using also the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem (see
[
28
, Theorem 1.153]
) there exists a subsequence
\rho_{n}\searrow 0
such that

(5.133)

\mu_{0}(\partial R_{\rho_{n}})=0,\qquad\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mu_{k}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})}=\mu_{0}(R_{\rho_{n}})

and

(5.134)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A)}(0)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\frac{\mu_{0}(R_{\rho_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}.

We now claim that

(5.135)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{h,K}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})

uniformly in
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}
as
n\to\infty
, where
H_{0}
is the half space centered in
0
with respect to the vector
\nu_{A}
. To prove the claim we can for example observe that by
[
36
, Proposition A.4]
we have (not only (b2), but also) that
\overline{Q_{r}}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(A)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{r}}\cap T_{x}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{Q_{r}}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}({S_{h,K}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{r}}\cap T_{x}\quad\text{as }\rho\to 0
for any square
Q_{r}
such that
R_{\nu_{A}}\subset Q_{r}

and hence, by Proposition
5.3
-(c) applied to
Q_{r}
,
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})
and
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{h,K}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})
uniformly in
{\overline{Q_{r}}}\supset R_{\nu_{A}}
.

Furthermore, from the
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence it follows that

(5.136)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A_{k})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A)\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{S_{k}})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{S_{h,K}})

uniformly in
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
and hence, by (
5.135
) and (
5.136
), a standard diagonalization argument yields that there exists a subsequence
\left\{(A_{k_{n}},h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}})\right\}
such that

(5.137)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0}),\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})

uniformly in
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}
as
n\to\infty
and by (
5.133
) such that

(5.138)

\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})\leq\mu_{0}(R_{\rho_{n}})+\rho_{n}^{2},

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We also observe that

(5.139)

\begin{split}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A)}(0)&\geq\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\\
&\geq c_{1}\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}},\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used (
4.1
)-(
4.5
), as
A_{k_{n}}
and
S_{k_{n}}
are sets of finite perimeter, and (
3.15
) while in the second inequality we used (
5.134
) and (
5.138
).

Now, we claim that

(5.140)

\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap(\overline{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}})))\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap T_{0}.

We proceed by contradiction, let
x_{n}\in\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap\overline{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))
such that
x_{n}\to x
and assume that
x\in{\rm{Int}}(\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap H_{0})
or
x\in R_{\nu_{A}}\setminus H_{0}
. In the first case, there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that
{\rm{sdist}}(x,\partial H_{0})=-\varepsilon
. By (
5.137
)
we observe that
{\rm{sdist}}(x,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))\to-\varepsilon
uniformly in
R_{\nu_{A}}
as
n\to\infty
. Furthermore, for
n
large enough,
x_{n}\in B_{{\varepsilon}/{2}}(x)
and it follows that
{\rm{sdist}}(x_{n},\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))
is negative. Therefore, for
n
large enough,
x_{n}\in{\rm{Int}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}})))

which is an absurd. Analogously if
x\in R_{\nu_{A}}\setminus H_{0}
we have that
{\rm{sdist}}(x,\partial H_{0})=\varepsilon
and by (
5.137
),
{\rm{sdist}}(x,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))\to\varepsilon
uniformly in
R_{\nu_{A}}
as
n\to\infty
, similarly as before, we can conclude, for
n
large enough, that
x_{n}\in R_{\nu_{A}}\setminus\overline{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}
, which is an absurd.

Now, let
x\in\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap T_{0}
. By Kuratowski convergence there exists
\{x_{n}\}\subset R_{\nu_{A}}\cap\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})
such that
x_{n}\to x
. We see that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
x_{n}\in R_{\nu_{A}}\cap\overline{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}
and
x_{n}\notin R_{\nu_{A}}\setminus{\rm{Int}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))
, if not, there exists
n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
x_{n^{\prime}}\in R_{\nu_{A}}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n^{\prime}}}(S_{k_{n^{\prime}}}))\subset\subset R_{\nu_{A}}\cap{\rm{Int}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n^{\prime}}}(A_{k_{n^{\prime}}}))
, which is an absurd.

Since
\left\{(A_{k_{n}},S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\right\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{m}}
, we know that
(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(\Omega))
. In view of (
5.137
) and (
5.140
) by applying Lemma
5.7
to
(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})})
and
R_{\nu_{A}}
,
with
\phi_{\alpha}(\cdot)=\varphi_{\alpha}(0,\cdot)
for
\alpha=\text{F,S,FS}
, and by fixing
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, there exists
n^{1}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
n\geq n^{1}_{\varepsilon}
,

(5.141)

\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})},R_{\nu_{A}})\geq\int_{T_{0}\cap\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}}{\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\varepsilon\geq 2\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})-\varepsilon.\end{split}

Moreover, by the uniform continuity of the Finsler norm
\varphi_{\alpha}
for
\alpha=\text{F,S,FS}
there exists
n^{2}_{\varepsilon}\geq n^{1}_{\epsilon}
such that

(5.142)

\begin{split}\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})&\geq\mu_{k_{n}}(R_{\rho_{n}})\\
&\geq\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}A_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial S_{k_{n}}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}}(0))\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap(\partial A_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial S_{k_{n}})\cap\left(A_{k_{n}}^{(0)}\cup A_{k_{n}}^{(1)}\right)}{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{S_{k_{n}}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k_{n}}}}{\varphi(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}(0)})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap(\partial^{*}{S_{k_{n}}}\setminus\partial{A_{k_{n}}})\cap A_{k_{n}}^{(1)}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(0,\nu_{S_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap(\partial{S_{k_{n}}}\cap\partial{A_{k_{n}}})\cap\left(S_{k_{n}}^{(1)}\cup A_{k_{n}}^{(0)}\right)}{2\varphi(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial{S_{k_{n}}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k_{n}}}\cap S_{k_{n}}^{(0)}}{(\varphi+\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}})(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap(\partial{S_{k_{n}}}\setminus\partial{A_{k_{n}}})\cap\left(S_{k_{n}}^{(1)}\cup S_{k_{n}}^{(0)}\right)\cap A_{k_{n}}^{(1)}}{2\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}}(0,\nu_{S_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{S_{k_{n}}}\cap\partial{A_{k_{n}}}\cap A_{k_{n}}^{(1)}}{(\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}+\varphi)(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{R_{\rho_{n}}\cap(\partial{S_{k_{n}}}\cap\partial{A_{k_{n}}})\cap S_{k_{n}}^{(0)}\cap A_{k_{n}}^{(1)}}{(\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}+\varphi+\varphi_{\mathrm{FS}})(0,\nu_{S_{k_{n}}}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right),\\
&=:{\mathcal{S}}_{L}({A_{k_{n}}},S_{h_{k_{n}},{K_{k_{n}}}},R_{\rho_{n}})-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right)\\
\end{split}

for every
n\geq n^{2}_{\varepsilon}
, where in the first inequality we used the definition of
\mu_{k_{n}}
and in the second inequality (
5.127
), and hence,

(5.143)

\begin{split}\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})&\geq\rho_{n}\left({\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})},R_{\nu_{A}})\right)\\
&\qquad-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right)\\
&\geq 2\rho_{n}\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})-\varepsilon\rho_{n}-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right),\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used (
5.142
) and the properties of the blow up map, and in the last inequality we used (
5.141
).
Finally, we conclude that

(5.144)

\begin{split}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A)}(0)&\geq\liminf_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\\
&\geq\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\varepsilon\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\\
&\geq\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{1}}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}S_{h,K}\cap\partial^{*}A)}(0),\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used (
5.138
), in the second inequality we used (
5.143
) and in the last inequality we used (
5.139
). By (b3) and taking
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
, in the inequality above, we deduce (
5.116
).
∎

Proof of (
5.117
).

By the
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-rectifiability of
\partial A
, by
[
36
, Proposition A.4]
(applied with
K
taken as
\partial A
), and by the Besicovitch Derivation theorem, the set of points
x\in(\partial A\setminus\partial S_{h,K})\cap A^{(1)}
not satisfying the following 4 conditions:

(c1)

\theta^{*}(\partial A,x)=\theta_{*}(\partial A,x)=1
,

(c2)

\nu_{A}(x)
exists,

(c3)

\overline{Q_{1,\nu_{A}(x)}}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(A)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1,\nu_{A}(x)}}\cap T_{x,\nu_{A}(x)}
as
\rho\to 0
,

(c4)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=4.82224pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.39182pt\vrule height=0.39182pt,depth=0.0pt,width=3.91806pt}((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)})}\left(x\right)
exists and it is finite,

is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible. Therefore, we prove (
5.117
) for any fixed
x\in(\partial A\setminus\partial S_{h,K})\cap A^{(1)}
satisfying (c1)-(c4). Without loss of generality we assume that
x=0
and
\nu_{A}(0)=\mathbf{e}_{\mathbf{1}}
, and we use the notation
T_{0}:=T_{0,\nu_{A}(0)}
. Again by the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem there exists a subsequence
\rho_{n}\searrow 0
such that

(5.145)

\mu_{0}(\partial Q_{\rho_{n}})=0,\quad\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mu_{k}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})}=\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})

and

(5.146)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)})}(0)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\frac{\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}.

By (c3) and applying Proposition
5.3
-(a) to
A
we have that

(5.147)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A))\rightarrow-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})

uniformly in
\overline{Q_{1}}
as
n\to\infty
. Furthermore, from the
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence it follows that

(5.148)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A_{k})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A)

uniformly in
\overline{Q_{1}}
as
k\to\infty
and hence, by (
5.147
) and (
5.148
), a standard diagonalization argument yields that there exists a subsequence
\left\{(A_{k_{n}},S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\right\}
such that

(5.149)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))\rightarrow-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0}).

uniformly in
\overline{Q_{1}}
as
n\to\infty
and, by also using (
5.145
), such that

(5.150)

\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})\leq\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})+\rho_{n}^{2},

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By arguing as in (
5.139
), we infer that

(5.151)

\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\leq c_{1}^{-1}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)})}(0).

By (
5.149
) and by applying Lemma
5.2
, we have that
\overline{Q_{1}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{n}}{(A_{k_{n}})}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0}
as
n\to\infty
. Since the number of connected components of
\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})
lying inside of
Q_{1}
does not exceed
m_{1}
, by
[
36
, Lemma 4.5]
(applied by taking
m_{0},\delta,\phi
in the notation of
[
36
, Lemma 4.5]
as
m_{1}
,
\varepsilon
, and
\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\cdot)
, respectively) implies that there exists
n^{1}_{\varepsilon}\geq n_{\varepsilon}
such that for every
n\geq n^{1}_{\varepsilon}
,

(5.152)

\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{1}\cap\partial^{*}\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}{\hskip-12.80365pt\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}(0))\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\qquad+\int_{Q_{1}\cap\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\cap((\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))^{(0)}\cup(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))^{(1)})}{\hskip-12.80365pt2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\geq 2\int_{\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\mathbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\varepsilon=4\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\mathbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})-\varepsilon.\end{split}

In view of Remark
3.8
-(iii) and by
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence, there exists a ball
B_{r(0)}(0)
and
n^{2}_{\varepsilon}\geq n^{1}_{\varepsilon}
such that

Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\subset B_{r(0)}(0)\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}=\emptyset
for any
n\geq n^{2}_{\varepsilon}
, and thus,

(5.153)

\emptyset=Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}=\rho_{n}(Q_{1}\cap\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}})).

Therefore, by (
5.152
) and (
5.153
), we obtain that

(5.154)

\begin{split}&\int_{Q_{1}\cap(\partial^{*}\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\setminus\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})})\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\quad+\int_{Q_{1}\cap(\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\setminus\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))\cap((\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))^{(0)}\cup(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))^{(1)})}{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})}(0))\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\\
&\geq 4\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\mathbf{e}_{2})-\varepsilon.\end{split}

Furthermore, by (
5.127
) it follows that there exists
n^{3}_{\varepsilon}\geq n^{2}_{\varepsilon}
such that

\displaystyle\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})

\displaystyle\geq\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap\partial^{*}{A_{k_{n}}}\setminus{\partial{S_{k_{n}}}}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{Q_{\rho_{n}}\cap(\partial A_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial S_{k_{n}})\cap(A_{k_{n}}^{(0)}\cup A_{k_{n}}^{(1)})}{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{A_{k_{n}}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right)

\displaystyle=\rho_{n}\left(\int_{Q_{1}\cap(\partial^{*}\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\setminus\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\left.\int_{Q_{1}\cap(\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\setminus\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}}))\cap((\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))^{(0)}\cup(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))^{(1)})}{2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right)

(5.155)

\displaystyle\geq 4\rho_{n}\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\mathbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})-\varepsilon\rho_{n}-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right),

for every
n\geq n^{3}_{\varepsilon}
, where in the first inequality we argued as in (
5.142
) (with
Q_{\rho_{n}}
instead of
R_{\rho_{n}}
) and we used the non-negativeness of
\psi_{k_{n}}
, in the equality we used properties of the blow up map, and in the second inequality we used (
5.154
). Finally, by (
5.146
), (
5.150
) and (
5.155
) and by repeating the same arguments of (
5.144
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)})}(0)

\displaystyle\geq 2\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\mathbf{e}_{\mathbf{1}})-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{1}}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial A\setminus\partial{S_{h,K}})\cap A^{(1)})}(0).

By (c4) and taking
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
, in the inequality above, we deduce (
5.117
).
∎

Proof of (
5.120
).

By the
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-rectifiability of
\partial A
and
\partial{S_{h,K}}
, by

, Proposition A.4]
(applied with
K
taken as first
\partial A
and then
\partial S_{h,K}
), and by the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem the set of points

not satisfying the following 4 conditions:

(f1)

\theta^{*}(\partial A,x)=\theta_{*}(\partial A,x)=\theta^{*}(\partial{S_{h,K}},x)=\theta_{*}(\partial{S_{h,K}},x)=1

\overline{Q_{1,\nu_{A}\left(x\right)}(x)}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(A)\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1,\nu_{A}\left(x\right)}(x)}\cap T_{x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right)}
and
\overline{Q_{1,\nu_{A}\left(x\right)}(x)}\cap\partial{\sigma}_{\rho,x}(S_{h,K})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1,\nu_{A}\left(x\right)}(x)}\cap T_{x,\nu_{A}\left(x\right)}
,

(f4)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=4.82224pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.39182pt\vrule height=0.39182pt,depth=0.0pt,width=3.91806pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K})}\left(x\right)
exists and it is finite.

is
\mathcal{H}^{1}
-negligible. Therefore we prove (
5.120
) for any fixed
x\in(\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K}
satisfying
(f1)-(f4). By (f2) and without loss of generality we assume that
x=0
and
\nu_{A}(0)=\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}}
, and we denote

.
Again by the Besicovitch Derivation Theorem there exists a subsequence
\rho_{n}\searrow 0
such that

(5.156)

\mu_{0}(\partial Q_{\rho_{n}})=0,\quad\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mu_{k}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})}=\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})

and

(5.157)

\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K})}(0)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\frac{\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}.

By (f3) and applying Proposition
5.3
-(a) to
A
and
S_{h,K}
we have that

(5.158)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A))\rightarrow-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{h,K}}))\rightarrow-{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,T_{0})

uniformly in

as

.
Furthermore, from the

-convergence it follows that

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A_{k})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A)\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{S_{k}})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{S_{h,K}})

uniformly in
\overline{Q_{1}}
as

and hence, by (

) and (

uniformly in
\overline{Q_{1}}
as
n\to\infty
and by (
5.156
) such that

(5.161)

\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})\leq\mu_{0}(Q_{\rho_{n}})+\rho_{n}^{2},

for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By arguing as in (
5.139
) we deduce that

(5.162)

\begin{split}\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\leq c_{1}^{-1}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K})}(0).\end{split}

By (
5.160
) and by applying Lemma
5.2
we deduce that

(5.163)

{\overline{Q_{1}}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad{\overline{Q_{1}}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0}.

Since
\left\{(A_{k_{n}},S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\right\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{m}}
, we know that
(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(\Omega))
.

5.163

5.9
to

\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}_{L}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})},Q_{1})&\geq 2\int_{\overline{Q_{1}}\cap T_{0}}{\varphi(0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})\,\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\varepsilon\geq 4\varphi(0,\textbf{e}_{\textbf{1}})-\varepsilon.\end{split}

\displaystyle\geq 2\varphi(0,\mathbf{e}_{\mathbf{1}})-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}-\frac{\varepsilon}{c_{1}}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K})}(0).

Proof of (
5.125
).

By the definition of
\partial^{*}S_{h,K}

, by
[

(applied with

) for any fixed

We now claim that

(5.168)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A))\rightarrow-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{h,K}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})

uniformly in

as

. To prove the claim we can for example observe that by

, Proposition A.4]
we have (not only (h3), but also) that

for any square
Q_{r}
such that
R_{\nu_{A}}\subset Q_{r}

and hence, by Proposition
5.3
Items (a) and (c), applied to
Q_{r}
,
{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A))\rightarrow-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{h,K}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})
uniformly in
\overline{Q_{r}}\supset R_{\nu_{A}}
.

Furthermore, from the
\tau_{\mathcal{B}}
-convergence it follows that

(5.169)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A_{k})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial A)\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{S_{k}})\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial{S_{h,K}})

uniformly in
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
and hence,
by (
5.168
) and (
5.169
), a standard diagonalization argument yields that there exists a subsequence
\left\{(A_{k_{n}},h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}})\right\}
such that

(5.170)

{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}}))\rightarrow-{\rm dist}(\cdot,T_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{{k_{n}}}}))\rightarrow{\rm{sdist}}(\cdot,\partial H_{0})

uniformly in
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}
as
n\to\infty
and by (
5.166
) such that

(5.171)

\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}})\leq\mu_{0}(R_{\rho_{n}})+\rho_{n}^{2},

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By (
5.166
), (
5.171
) and arguing as in (

) we infer that

(5.172)

\begin{split}\limsup_{n\to\infty}{\frac{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{R_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})}{2\rho_{n}}}\leq c_{1}^{-1}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial^{*}{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A\cap A^{(1)})}(0).\end{split}

By applying Lemma
5.2
, we have that
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\setminus\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(A_{k_{n}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}
and in view of Remark

-(i) we have that
\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap\partial\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(S_{k_{n}})\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}T_{0}
. Since
\left\{(A_{k_{n}},S_{h_{k_{n}},K_{k_{n}}})\right\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\mathbf{m}}
, we know that
(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{(1/{\rho_{n}})h_{k_{n}}(\rho_{n}\cdot),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})})\in\mathcal{B}_{\textbf{m}}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}(\Omega))
. By applying Lemma
5.8
with
\phi_{\alpha}(\cdot)=\varphi_{\alpha}(0,\cdot)
for
\alpha=\text{F,S,FS}
and

\delta=\varepsilon
, there exists
n^{1}_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every

,

(5.173)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{L}(\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({A_{k_{n}}}),S_{\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({S_{k_{n}}}),\sigma_{\rho_{n}}({K_{k_{n}}})},R_{\nu_{A}})&\geq\int_{\overline{R_{\nu_{A}}}\cap T_{0}}{\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{T_{0}}))+\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}}-\varepsilon\\
&=2(\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{T_{0}})+\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}}))-\varepsilon.\end{split}

By definition of

), (

) and arguing as in (

\mu_{k_{n}}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}})\geq 2(\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{T_{0}})+\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}}))-\varepsilon\rho_{n}-\varepsilon\left(\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial A_{k_{n}})+\mathcal{H}^{1}(\overline{Q_{\rho_{n}}}\cap\partial S_{k_{n}}\setminus\partial A_{k_{n}})\right),

for every

. By (

), (

), (

) and by arguing as in

) we have that

\begin{split}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}(\partial A\cap\partial^{*}S\cap A^{(1)})}(0)&\geq\varphi_{\mathrm{F}}(0,\nu_{T_{0}})+\varphi(0,\nu_{T_{0}})-\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad-\varepsilon c_{1}^{-1}\frac{d\mu_{0}}{\,d\mathcal{H}^{1}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.88889pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=5.59721pt}((\partial{S_{h,K}}\cap\partial A)\cap S^{(1)}_{h,K})}(0).\end{split}

Finally, by (h4) and by taking

in the inequality above, we deduce (

.

Proof of Theorem
3.12
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that the
liminf
in the right side of (
3.22
) is reached and finite in
\mathbb{R}
. From Theorem
5.13
it follows that

(5.175)

\mathcal{S}(A,S_{h,K})\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow\infty}{\mathcal{S}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}})}.

In view of the definition of
\mathcal{F}
, in order to reach the assertion, it suffices to establish the lower semicontinuity of
\mathcal{W}
, to which the rest of the proof is devoted.

Let
D\subset\subset\textrm{Int}(A)
, by the fact that
{\rm{Int}}{(A_{k})}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{K}}{\rm{Int}}{(A)}
, we deduce that
D\subset\subset{\rm{Int}}(A_{k})
for
k
large enough. As
u_{k}\rightarrow u
a.e. in
{\rm{Int}}(A)
, then,
u_{k}\rightarrow u
a.e. in
D
. Furthermore, since
e(u_{k})
are bounded in the
L^{2}(D)
norm, we have that
e(u_{k})\rightharpoonup e(u)
in
L^{2}(D)
. By convexity of
\mathcal{W}(D,\cdot)
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(D,u)\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mathcal{W}(D,u_{k})}\leq\liminf_{k\rightarrow+\infty}{\mathcal{W}(A_{k},u_{k})}.

To conclude it is now enough to let
D\nearrow{\rm{Int}}(A)
.
∎

6.
Existence

In view of Theorems
3.11
and
3.12
we are in position to prove Theorem
3.10
by employing the
direct method of the calculus of variations
.

Proof of Theorem
3.10
.

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\{(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},u_{k})\}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
be a minimizing sequence of
\mathcal{F}
such that
\mathcal{L}^{2}({A_{k}})=\mathbbm{v}_{1}
,
\mathcal{L}^{2}({S_{h_{k},K_{k}}})=\mathbbm{v}_{0}
, and

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{F}(A_{k},S_{h_{k},K_{k}},u_{k})}<\infty.

By Theorem
3.11
there exist a subsequence
\{(A_{k_{l}},S_{h_{k_{l}},K_{k_{l}}},u_{k_{l}})\}
, a sequence
\{(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l})\}\subset\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
, and
(A,S_{h,K},u)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}}
such that
(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\mathcal{C}}}(A,S_{h,K},u)
as
l\to\infty
and

(6.1)

\liminf_{l\to\infty}{\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l})}=\liminf_{l\to\infty}{\mathcal{F}(A_{k_{l}},S_{h_{k_{l}},K_{k_{l}}},u_{k_{l}})}.

By Theorem
3.12
, we have that

(6.2)

\mathcal{F}(A,S_{h,K},u)\leq\liminf_{l\to\infty}{\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l})}.

We claim that
\{(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l})\}
and
(A,S_{h,K},u)
satisfy the volume constraints of (
3.18
). Indeed, by Theorem
3.11
, for any
l\geq 1
,
\mathbbm{v}_{1}=\mathcal{L}^{2}\left({A_{k_{l}}}\right)=\mathcal{L}^{2}({\widetilde{A}_{l}})
and
\mathbbm{v}_{0}=\mathcal{L}^{2}({S_{h_{k_{l}},{K}_{k_{l}}}})=\mathcal{L}^{2}\left({S_{h_{l},\widetilde{K}_{l}}}\right)
, where
{S_{h_{l},\widetilde{K}_{l}}}\in{\rm AS}(\Omega)
. Thanks to the fact that
(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},K_{k_{l}}})\xrightarrow{\tau_{\mathcal{\mathcal{B}}}}(A,S_{h,K})
as
l\to\infty
, by applying
[
36
, Lemma 3.2]
we infer that
\widetilde{A}_{l}\to A
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
as
l\to\infty
, and thus
\mathcal{L}^{2}({A})=\mathbbm{v}_{1}
, and, similarly, we deduce that
\mathcal{L}^{2}({S_{h,K}})=\mathbbm{v}_{0}
. Finally, from (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) we deduce that

\begin{split}\inf_{(A,S_{h,K},u)\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{m}},\,\mathcal{L}^{2}({A})=\mathbbm{v}_{1},\,\mathcal{L}^{2}({S_{h,K}})=\mathbbm{v}_{0}}\mathcal{F}(A,S_{h,K},u)&=\lim_{k\to\infty}{\mathcal{F}(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l})}\\
&\geq\liminf_{l\to\infty}{\mathcal{F}\left(\widetilde{A}_{l},S_{h_{k_{l}},\widetilde{K}_{k_{l}}},v_{l}\right)}\\
&\geq\mathcal{F}(A,S_{h,K},u)\end{split}

and hence,
(A,S_{h,K},u)
is a solution of the minimum problem (
3.18
). By observing that (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) hold true also by replacing
\mathcal{F}
with
\mathcal{F}^{\lambda}
for
{\lambda}:=(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}),
with
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}>0
, we deduce that also the unconstrained minimum problem (
3.19
) can be solved by employing the same method. This concludes the proof.
∎

*
